4. Player identity is authoritative world state, serialized in saves, validated
   on load, and re-created after a worker restart for the remembered country.
5. The AI phase skips `world.playerCountryId`.

### Consequences

- No implicit "country 0".
- A save from another scenario is rejected by the scenario-id check.
- A rejected selection leaves the previous world intact and reports the error.

## D-090 — Bounded country detail in snapshots

**Status:** implemented (M11). Protocol 14.

### Context

A full `CountrySnapshot` is about 35–40 KiB. Sixty full records exceed 2 MiB per
snapshot, which is impractical for a 10 Hz presentation bus.

### Decision

1. `SimulationSnapshot.countries` carries full detail only for the player country
   and the focused country.
2. `countrySummaries` carries a compact record for every country: identity,
   status, sovereign, capital, region/market ids, population, employment,
   treasury, hardship, literacy, wealth, militancy, military personnel and the
   last closed net fiscal result.
3. `SET_FOCUS` is presentation-only and changes which country's full record is
   included. It is validated against the world but never mutates simulation
   state.
4. Legacy scenarios without catalog metadata keep the pre-M11 all-countries
   behavior so existing fixtures and tests remain meaningful.

### Consequences

- The M11 snapshot is about 200 KiB instead of about 2.5 MiB.
- Screens that inspect an AI country rely on the focus round-trip; the map/list
  always has summary data and never blocks on focus.
- No serialized world field changed, so world schema remains 26.

## D-091 — Market identity is authored from actual membership

**Status:** implemented (M11).

### Context

The snapshot previously labelled markets `Market 1`, `Market 2`, …; this is an
opaque id, not a player-facing identity, and it hid multi-country membership.

### Decision

1. The catalog authors 18 markets with display name, kind and description
   derived from their real member countries and regions.
2. `MarketSnapshot` exposes `name`, `description`, `kind`, `memberCountryIds`,
   `regionIds`, customs administration and prices.
3. The snapshot builder never falls back to `Market N`: without authored metadata
   it derives a name from the member countries.
4. Market screens list every member country and region and distinguish customs
   administration from sovereignty. The numeric id is secondary diagnostic text.
5. Market membership is not assumed to equal one country. Imperial markets
   include dependent territories; shared markets include several countries.

### Consequences

- A market is discoverable from the map, the country panel and the Markets
  screen.
- The one-market-per-country invariant is preserved; see D-087.

## D-092 — Offline map geometry, licensing and projection

**Status:** implemented (M11).

### Context

M11 requires a real historical map that works offline. Historical vector
datasets were reviewed for licensing and compatibility.

### Decision

1. Geometry is Natural Earth 1:50m (public domain) via world-atlas 2.0.2 TopoJSON
   (ISC), aggregated into 1900 polities by the catalog crosswalk.
2. CShapes 2.0 (CC BY-NC-SA 4.0) is consulted only for classification and is not
   redistributed. historical-basemaps (GPL-3.0, copyleft) is not used.
3. Build-time union uses `polygon-clipping` 0.15.7 (MIT). Source polygons are
   simplified to 0.02°, quantized to 0.001°, delta encoded, and unwrapped at the
   antimeridian.
4. South Africa is split using Natural Earth admin-1 provinces; Newfoundland is a
   marker territory because the source cannot separate it from Canada.
5. The renderer uses a Miller cylindrical projection with linear longitude, so
   each ring can be drawn at −360/0/+360 offsets for the seam.
6. The map is bundled. The game never fetches map data or map tiles at runtime;
   no API keys or proprietary services are used.
7. `react-native-svg` 15.16.0 (MIT), the Expo 58 bundled version, renders the
   map on web and native.

### Consequences

- The map works offline and the licence permits redistribution.
- 1900 borders are a crosswalk aggregation, not a historical GIS dataset; the
  limits are documented in §17I.12.
- Build scripts download the sources once into `.cache-m11`; the committed
  bundles are the runtime artifacts.

## D-093 — Slider-first continuous controls with coalesced commits

**Status:** implemented (M11).

### Context

The pre-M11 screens used text fields and quick-set buttons for continuous policy
values, which is imprecise, inaccessible and easy to race against the worker.

### Decision

1. Continuous numeric player controls use `SliderField`: visible value, min/max,
   step, unit, ticks, live preview and a paired numeric input.
2. Sliders support mouse, touch, keyboard step buttons, the platform's
   accessible increment/decrement actions, and typed precision.
3. Commits are coalesced by `useDeferredCommit`: one authoritative action is in
   flight at a time and the latest value wins. Sliders are disabled while the
   worker is unavailable.
4. Categorical, boolean, date, speed, market/country and confirmation inputs are
   explicitly excluded; the inventory is `docs/M11_SLIDER_INVENTORY.md`.
5. Every slider value maps to a validated action through the existing
   `APPLY_ACTION` boundary; the UI never mutates simulation internals.

### Consequences

- Policy changes are always authoritative actions, with previews remaining
  local.
- The slider ranges are constrained to values the authoritative validator
  accepts; endpoints and out-of-range cases are covered by tests.
- Some low-level derived values (for example contract ids) remain read-only.

## D-094 — Curated historical flag system keyed by polity ID

**Status:** implemented (M11 flag feature).

### Context

The 1900 catalog had no flag artwork: every polity was represented by the
existing monogram (national colour plus two-letter tag). The flag feature
requires historically correct 1 January 1900 flags, PD/CC0-only artwork,
offline runtime behaviour, and no change to simulation behaviour or the
political definitions of the catalog.

### Decision

1. The curated approval list is
   `packages/data/data/historical/m11/flags_1900.json`. It records, per flag,
   the Commons file, licence and licence code, author, source URLs, source
   SHA-1 and dimensions, validity window, retrieval date and historical notes;
   and per polity, a flag ID or an explicit fallback reason plus a basis
   (`national`, `sovereign-flag`, `documented-exception`, `fallback`).
2. Flags are resolved only through stable numeric polity IDs
   (`POLITY_FLAG`), never display names. `CountryFlag` takes `polityId` and
   renders the monogram when the mapping has no asset.
3. Every file was checked individually through the Wikimedia Commons API.
   Only licence code `pd` (Public Domain, including PD-old/PD-self/PD-ineligible)
   or `cc0` is accepted by the pipeline; CC BY, CC BY-SA and GFDL are rejected.
4. `scripts/m11/build-flags.mjs` retrieves the approved Commons renditions at a
   normalized 500 px width (preserving each flag's own ratio), converts the one
   approved JPEG source to PNG, validates the PNG structure and writes the
   committed assets, ledger, generated mapping, generated provenance module and
   `docs/FLAG_PROVENANCE.md`. `pnpm flags:check` is offline and verifies the
   committed hashes and generated outputs; `pnpm flags:audit` re-verifies the
   live Commons licences. Normal builds never run the network path.
5. `jimp-compact@0.16.1` is added as a root **devDependency** solely for the
   build-time JPEG-to-PNG conversion of the CC0 1886 Qajar flag. It has no
   runtime or bundle impact.
6. Monogram fallbacks: Spain (ESP) and its colonies SAH/SGG (no PD/CC0 artwork
   of the 1875–1931 state flag with the Restoration arms), Montenegro (MNT;
   national flag not standardised in 1900 and the only PD file is the war
   ensign) and the Transvaal (ZAR; the Vierkleur artwork is CC BY-SA).
7. Canada uses the 1868–1921 Red Ensign as the distinctive de facto flag, with
   the Union Jack's continuing official status documented. Australia and New
   Zealand use the Union Jack (federation on 1 January 1901; New Zealand's own
   ensign dates from 1902).
8. Dependent territories use the sovereign's flag plus the existing status
   badge. Egypt is the single documented exception (its own khedival flag,
   because it was an autonomous Ottoman province with a recognised flag, not a
   colony).
9. Austria-Hungary is absent from the existing 1900 catalog. The catalog was
   deliberately not changed; the absence and the correct civil-ensign nuance
   are recorded in `docs/FLAG_PROVENANCE.md`.

### Consequences

- The feature is presentation-only: no simulation state, data version,
  simulation version, determinism or snapshot contract changes.
- The app bundle carries committed PNG assets; displaying a flag never touches
  the network.
- 56 flags are bundled and 5 polities use the documented monogram fallback
  (ESP, MNT, ZAR, SAH, SGG).
- Adding an Austria-Hungary polity later requires a flag manifest and
  provenance regeneration.
- *M11b supersedes the counts and fallbacks above; see D-095.*

## D-095 — M11b complete 1900 catalog and monogram removal

**Status:** implemented (M11b).

### Context

The M11 catalog was derived from the GWD/COW independent-state tradition but
omitted Austria-Hungary entirely, did not separately represent several de facto
or autonomous 1900 polities, and left five documented monogram fallbacks
(Spain, Spanish Sahara, Spanish Guinea, Montenegro and the Transvaal). The M11b
task requires a systematic regional inventory, actual in-game additions, map
integration, full flag artwork and a formal coverage audit.

### Decision

1. **Inventory scope.** The audit reconciles 60 `independent`-status states,
   including four de facto/partially recognised African/Arabian states (Sokoto,
   Wadai, Darfur, Jabal Shammar), plus six other self-governing or occupied
   states already present (Bulgaria, Norway, Canada, Australia, New Zealand,
   Cuba) and one added autonomous state (Crete). The full sources, contested
   cases and omissions are in `docs/1900_CATALOG_COVERAGE.md`.
2. **Austria-Hungary is one state (ID 60), not two.** Its catalog entry records
   the dual structure, the Austrian and Hungarian halves, the joint ministries,
   the separate parliaments, the jointly administered Bosnia and the co-capital.
   Its flag is the common civil ensign because there was no single unified
   national flag.
3. **Stable IDs.** New states use 60–66 and new territories use 162–165. No
   existing polity ID is renumbered. Existing saves remain loadable: the save
   carries its original world, static metadata is looked up by `countryId`, and
   the additive entries do not alter existing IDs, tags or region ownership.
4. **Map integration.** Austria-Hungary uses a polygon of its core modern units.
   Polities without reliable 1900 polygon data use the existing marker-only map
   mechanism at their capitals, marked `markerOnly`/`approximate`; no borders are
   invented and no polygons overlap.
5. **Flags.** Spain and the Transvaal Vierkleur are original Century
   reconstructions; Spain composites an individually verified PD coat-of-arms
   component and the project owns the composition. Montenegrin, Austrian,
   Tongan, Cretan, Sokotan and Ha'il flags are individually verified PD Commons
   files. Project artwork is recorded separately from its historical sources.
   Wadai and Darfur remain documented monogram exceptions because no verified
   historical flag design could be established.
6. **Markets.** Two markets are added: `austro-hungarian` and `pacific`.
   Sokoto/Wadai/Darfur join `african-independent`; Jabal Shammar and Crete join
   `ottoman-egyptian`.
7. **Data/versioning.** Scenario content changes, so the generated tables,
   scenario and worker bundle are regenerated. The data version remains
   `m3-economy-v12`, world schema 26 and save format 1 because no authoritative
   state shape, unit, formula or save field changed.

### Consequences

- Final catalog: **67 states**, **66 territories**, **133 regions**,
  **20 markets**, **66 selectable states**.
- Flag assets: **64** (62 external PD/CC0, 2 project reconstructions); 131 of 133
  polities display a flag; only Wadai and Darfur use the monogram.
- The new African and Arabian states, Crete and Tonga have low-confidence
  starting data; they are playable but explicitly provisional.
- Austria-Hungary's Galicia, Transylvania, Vojvodina and South Tyrol are included
  in its population but not its core map polygon; the limitation is documented.
- New marker-only polities are fully selectable and picker-integrated but not
  drawn as territories until reliable 1900 polygons are available.

## D-096 — M11c historical geography, border reconstruction and representation audit

**Status:** implemented (M11c).

### Context

M11/M11b aggregated modern Natural Earth units into 1900 polities. That left
Libya as an unassigned void, kept modern German borders, and preserved many
post-1900 borders (Alsace-Lorraine inside France, Congress Poland inside Russia,
Austria-Hungary's lost provinces outside its polygon, Siam's ceded provinces
outside Siam, the African sultanates as markers, etc.). M11c requires a
systematic historical-border audit of every catalogued polity, not a modern
border with a few fragments added.

### Decision

1. **Modern units are building blocks only.** Base geometry remains Natural
   Earth 1:50m/world-atlas; 1:10m admin-1/admin-0 units are added as
   reconstruction blocks. No modern unit is accepted as a 1900 border without
   an explicit transfer or a documented verification.
2. **Explicit, sourced transfers.** Every territorial change is an authored
   record in `boundaries_1900.json` (destination, source polities, source units
   or polygon, optional clip/subtract, dated sources, confidence and notes).
   The build clips each transfer to its source polity, applies additions and
   removals with `polygon-clipping`, and resolves residual overlaps
   deterministically (destination beats source; otherwise the smaller polity
   keeps the sliver).
3. **Germany is the German Empire (1900), not the Federal Republic.** The
   reconstruction includes Alsace-Lorraine, North Schleswig, Eupen-Malmedy,
   Memel, East Prussia/Kaliningrad, and the Prussian eastern provinces clipped
   to the authored 1900 Russian/Austrian frontier. The frontier strip is MEDIUM
   confidence; the provinces are HIGH.
4. **Libya is Ottoman, not a modern Libyan state.** The modern Libyan outer
   frame is assigned to OTT, minus the Aouzou strip (left with Chad). The map
   provenance records Tripolitania, Cyrenaica and Fezzan separately, including
   the limits of effective control. No `LBY` polity is added.
5. **No marker-only polities remain.** Tonga, Sokoto, Wadai, Darfur, Jabal
   Shammar, Crete, Tibet, Khiva, Bukhara and Sarawak receive reconstructed
   polygons with explicit LOW/MEDIUM confidence. They remain simulation
   entries with unchanged IDs.
6. **Provenance is part of the map artifact.** Every polity entry carries the
   reference date, sources with URLs/licences, geometry class, confidence,
   underlying boundary date, deviations from the modern map, known
   uncertainties, transfer ids and optional composite components.
7. **Audit is generated and tested.** `audit-map.mjs` writes
   `world_map_1900_audit.json`; `m11c-geography.test.ts` checks validity,
   point-in-polygon history, provenance completeness, German/Libyan coverage,
   the absence of marker-only entries, the two documented residual coastline
   slivers and the absence of multi-sample land voids.
8. **No authoritative simulation change.** Polity IDs, tags, populations,
   markets, schema, save format and deterministic behavior are unchanged. The
   M11c work is scenario/map presentation plus provenance documentation.

### Consequences

- The map now shows a historically bounded German Empire, an Ottoman Libya and
  reconstructed borders across Europe, Asia, Africa, the Americas and Oceania.
- Residual generalization artefacts are documented, not hidden: the audit
  reports Canada/Newfoundland (0.249 deg²) and Bolivia/Brazil (0.016 deg²)
  coastline slivers, and 24 single-sample 1° coastal/island gaps. There is no
  multi-sample terrestrial void.
- Wadai and Darfur retain monogram flags: the M11c research found no defensible
  1900 national/state flag, only modern reconstructions, military standards or
  post-1900 movement flags. Their territories are now drawn.
- The full per-polity audit table and sources are in
  `docs/HISTORICAL_MAP_PROVENANCE.md`; the coverage reconciliation is updated in
  `docs/1900_CATALOG_COVERAGE.md`; the flag investigation is in
  `docs/FLAG_PROVENANCE.md`.


## D-097 — M11c.1 Aouzou dispute, overlap resolution, rendering and zoom

**Status:** implemented (M11c.1).

### Context

M11c left the Aouzou strip with French Equatorial Africa, reported two residual
overlaps, used a 1° coverage sample that missed narrow coastlines and islands,
had a stale land layer outside the zoom transform, and stopped at scale 10.
The user also selected Darfur and Wadai flag designs.

### Decision

1. **Aouzou–Tibesti is a disputed map-only zone, not FEA and not OTT.** The 1899
   Anglo-French Declaration was a sphere-of-influence line; the ICJ record shows
   later instruments (1919, 1902, 1955) were needed to settle the boundary.
   France had not established continuous administration across Tibesti; Ottoman
   authority was claimed and intermittent; Toubou/Teda communities held practical
   authority. The zone is removed from every polity, carries explicit claims,
   administration notes and ICJ sources, and adds no playable polity. A second
   small zone records the undelimited Karakoram–Siachen frontier.
2. **Overlaps are extracted, recorded and trimmed.** The Canada/Newfoundland
   intersection (0.249 deg²) and Bolivia/Brazil intersection (0.016 deg²) are
   written to `overlapResolutions` with bbox, area, decision and historical note.
   The Labrador dispute and the 1927 Privy Council line are not treated as a
   settled 1900 border; Acre is Bolivian in 1900 and the Treaty of Petrópolis is
   not applied retroactively. Only the sliver is trimmed.
3. **Coverage audit is polygon-level and high-resolution.** The audit groups
   land rings into outer/hole polygons, subtracts the union of polities and
   disputed zones per 30×30° tile, and reports total unassigned area, connected
   components, intentional Antarctica, sub-0.02° generalization slivers and
   small-island detail gaps. The 1° sample remains as a cross-check.
4. **Rendering layer order is synchronized.** The land mask moved inside the
   country transform group; it now zooms and pans with the fills and cannot
   paint over the ocean at a different scale. Land uses the even-odd rule for
   inland water holes; border strokes scale inversely so they remain constant
   width.
5. **Maximum zoom is 18** (previous 10, 1.8×). Wheel/trackpad/pinch zoom uses a
   letterbox-aware focal point and bounded pan; the pure viewport math is tested.
6. **Darfur/Wadai flags.** The Pax Historia rasters were inspected but are not
   bundled because redistribution permission was not established. Century
   independently reconstructed the simple geometric designs (green-red-black
   triband with white crescent; white field with black border), classifies them
   as user-selected representative flags rather than exact historical national
   flags, and records the decision in the manifest, provenance module and docs.

### Consequences

- No monogram fallback remains; all 133 polities display a flag.
- The Aouzou/Tibesti area is visually a dashed amber uncertainty zone, not an
  unexplained void or an invented national territory.
- The map renders cleanly at scale 18; the stale white/land-over-ocean overlay is
  gone. Residual sub-0.01 deg² coastline slivers and small-island detail gaps are
  documented rather than cosmetically filled.
- Stable IDs, populations, markets, saves and simulation behavior are unchanged.

## D-098 — M11d runtime playtest fixes (worker lifecycle, snapshot bound, web input)

**Status:** implemented (M11d).

### Context

Playtesting the exported Expo web build (headless Chrome through CDP) found six
issues that static checks could not reach:

1. **Saving rebuilt the simulation.** `handleEvent` closed over the
   `savedCampaign` state, and the worker-creation effect listed the handlers as
   dependencies. Completing a save therefore tore down the Web Worker, then the
   provider re-created the campaign from the player identity, silently resetting
   the simulated date and discarding unsaved progress.
2. **A United Kingdom campaign could not start.** The app rejected the
   `NEW_GAME_RESULT`: the protocol validator bounded `constructionLabor.idleWorkers`
   at 100,000,000, while the valid UK pool is 107,781,153 (the United Kingdom plus
   British India). The generic error was "event.snapshot is not a valid snapshot".
3. **Wheel zoom logged an error on every notch.** React attaches `onWheel`
   passively, so the synthetic handler's `preventDefault()` produced "Unable to
   preventDefault inside passive event listener invocation" and the page could
   still scroll.
4. **Sliders exposed no value to screen readers on web.** The component set
   `accessibilityValue`, but React Native Web reads the flat `aria-value*` props;
   the rendered `role="slider"` had no `aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuemin`,
   `aria-valuemax` or `aria-valuetext`.
5. **An unreadable saved game looked like no saved game.** The startup storage
   error was cleared by the worker's initial `READY` event, leaving "No saved
   campaign yet" with no explanation for an incompatible or corrupt save.
6. **Every load requested a missing `/favicon.ico`.**
7. **Country labels swallowed map clicks.** SVG text labels sit at polity
   centroids and were the pointer target, so clicking a labelled country (for
   example British India) produced a hover tooltip but no selection.

### Decision

1. Worker lifecycle depends only on the explicit restart epoch (`webEpoch` /
   `nativeEpoch`). Supervisor callbacks always dispatch through refs, and the
   saved-campaign envelope is mirrored into a ref. No simulation or save-format
   change; identity and date are preserved across saves.
2. Add the documented `MAX_SNAPSHOT_PEOPLE = 20_000_000_000` bound for person
   counts in snapshots (the 1900 world starts at ~1.59bn and the campaign can run
   to 2000). The snapshot shape is unchanged, so `PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays 14; only
   the accepted value range of a valid person count widens.
3. The map owns a native, explicitly non-passive `wheel` listener on its
   container, maps `clientX`/`clientY` through the container box, and calls
   `preventDefault()`. The pure factor math is `wheelZoomFactor` in
   `mapViewport.ts`.
4. Sliders pass both `accessibilityValue` (native) and the flat `aria-value*`
   props (web), so a screen reader announces the current value and range.
5. `READY` clears an existing error only when no startup storage error is
   recorded; a successful new game or load clears the diagnosis.
6. `app.json` declares a bundled `web.favicon`; the export now emits
   `/favicon.ico` and the browser request succeeds.
7. Country labels are non-interactive (`pointerEvents="none"`); polity fills and
   small-state markers keep their press handlers.

### Consequences

- Saving, autosaving, loading and restarting no longer rebuild the worker
  implicitly; the observed worker count remains one per app session, and a
  20-second 30x run keeps its simulated date.
- Every selectable country's initial snapshot now passes the app protocol
  validator. `m11d-runtime-contract.test.ts` checks all 66 selectable countries
  through `NEW_GAME_RESULT` and `parseWorkerEvent`.
- Clicking a labelled country on the map now selects it instead of hitting the
  label. Labels remain visible and non-focusable.
- Pause is acknowledged only between worker pumps. The host may finish up to
  four queued ticks first, so at the full-world 30x rate the date can advance a
  few simulated days before the UI reports Play. This is the existing
  command-boundary contract, not a state regression; the playtest waits for the
  acknowledgement before comparing dates.
- No simulation behavior, determinism, save schema, stable IDs or data versions
  changed. The measured full-world cost is unchanged: about 650 ms per simulated
  day in Node, so 30x remains best-effort (documented in the M11d report).
- Native device builds were not available in this environment; the native
  provider path was hardened (same epoch rule) but not re-run on a device.

## D-099 — M11d follow-up: map seams, disputed-zone colour and country labels

**Status:** implemented (M11d follow-up).

### Context

Playtesting the M11d map reported four visual defects: a black square south of
Libya, a similar square near India, a straight line inside Germany close to East
Prussia/Memel, and country names that were only about five screen pixels high
and unreadable. Close inspection also showed a second coastline stroke offset
from the country coastline.

- The two "black squares" are the **Aouzou–Tibesti** and **Karakoram–Siachen**
  disputed frontier zones. They are deliberately excluded from every polity and
  were filled with `rgba(216,179,74,0.18)`; over the dark land mask that
  averaged to nearly black at world zoom.
- The line across East Prussia is the **1945 southern border of Kaliningrad
  Oblast**, a modern line that did not exist in 1900. The northern and southern
  East Prussian pieces were simplified independently and left a sub-0.02 degree
  gap, so the renderer kept two rings and stroked the modern line.
- The land mask and the polity border pass both stroked coastlines, using
  different simplification tolerances, producing two offset coastlines.

### Decision

1. **Display-only seam bridge.** `build-map.mjs` has a documented
   `DISPLAY_SEAM_BRIDGES` table. A thin strip along the Kaliningrad–Poland seam
   is unioned into the German geometry and the result is simplified again. The
   strip closes only the simplification gap; it adds ~0.025 deg² of the German
   land that the two pieces already represented. The correction is recorded in
   `world_map_1900.json` under `displayCorrections`; it changes no polity id,
   population, market, simulation state or save.
2. **Disputed zones are a hatch, not a dark translucent fill.** They use a
   diagonal amber pattern (`century-disputed-hatch`), a bright dashed edge and a
   "Disputed" label at regional zoom. They remain non-playable and unassigned.
3. **Country labels are sized to the country.** Screen font size is derived from
   the polity's projected bounding box, the length of its name, and its on-screen
   area, clamped to 7–24 px (selected +1.5 px). Labels use a dark halo, a
   collision cull and the player/selected country as a priority candidate.
4. **One coastline stroke.** The land mask is fill-only; the polity border pass
   is the single coastline source and is drawn at 1.1 px screen width with round
   joins/caps.
5. The map audit was regenerated after the geometry change.

### Consequences

- The Germany line near East Prussia/Memel is gone; Germany is eight outer rings
  (mainland, islands) with no artificial seam.
- The disputed zones read as amber hatched frontier annotations at world zoom and
  carry a label when zoomed in; they no longer look like black holes.
- Large countries get large names and small countries keep readable labels; the
  collision cull removes overlaps while the player/selected label is preserved.
- Coastlines are single-stroked; country borders are more visible.
- Map data only: DEU area +0.021 deg² and RUS −0.026 deg² from the display bridge
  and overlap resolution. Audit: 2 political overlaps (largest 0.004 deg²),
  4,047.8007 deg² unassigned (4,026.4191 deg² intentional Antarctica),
  `resolvedOverlaps` 201. No simulation, schema, RNG or save change.
- New regression suite `m11d-map-legibility.test.ts` and the updated map-selection
  tests cover the seam, hatch, label sizing and single coastline.

## D-100 — M11d follow-up: resolve the former disputed zones by current borders

**Status:** implemented (M11d follow-up; supersedes the disputed-zone rendering
in D-099).

### Context

D-099 left two map-only uncertainty rectangles (Aouzou–Tibesti and
Karakoram–Siachen) and rendered them as amber hatched annotations. The designer
then asked for no more disputed areas: choose a border and implement it, using
current borders to split the rectangles.

### Decision

1. `boundaries_1900.json` now gives each former zone a
   `resolution.strategy = "split-by-current-borders"` and a mapping from modern
   Natural Earth 1:50m admin-0 codes to 1900 polity tags:
   - Aouzou–Tibesti: `LBY → OTT`, `TCD → FEA`, `NER → FWA`, `DZA → ALG`
     (fallback FEA). Result: Chad/Aouzou/Tibesti → FEA (11.412 deg²), the
     northern edge → OTT (1.104 deg²), the western edge → FWA (1.484 deg²).
   - Karakoram–Siachen: `KAS → BIN`, `IND → BIN`, `PAK → BIN`, `CHN → CHN`
     (fallback BIN). Result: the Siachen Glacier, Indian and Pakistani polygons
     → British India (0.711 deg²), the Chinese side → Qing Empire (0.330 deg²).
   The current borders are a cartographic choice for a definite line, **not**
   1900 evidence.
2. The map build intersects each modern admin-0 polygon with the former
   rectangle, unions the intersection into the target polity and removes the
   rectangle from every polity first. The resolved components and source IDs are
   recorded in `world_map_1900.json → resolvedDisputes` and
   `meta.resolvedDisputeCount`.
3. `disputedAreas` is now empty. The disputed hatch, label and legend row were
   removed from `WorldMap.tsx`; the map draws ordinary sovereign fills and one
   border pass.
4. The map bundle and audit were regenerated. The boundary dataset version is
   `m11d-1900-boundaries-v2`.
5. Tests now assert the split components, point membership and the absence of
   disputed overlays; the audit reports `disputedAreas: 0`.

### Consequences

- The black squares are gone because there is no uncertainty overlay left; the
  area is ordinary territory of FEA/OTT/FWA and BIN/CHN.
- The border inside the former zones follows current Natural Earth borders. This
  is a deliberate, documented approximation and should not be read as a surveyed
  1900 frontier.
- No simulation polity, population, market, id, schema, RNG or save data
  changed. Map-only geometry and presentation changed.
- Audit: 133 polities, 0 invalid rings, 2 political overlaps (largest
  0.004 deg²), 0 disputed areas, 4,047.8074 deg² unassigned (4,026.4191 deg²
  intentional Antarctica).

## D-101 — M11e: acyclic simulation dependency hierarchy

**Status:** implemented (M11e).

### Context

The Expo/Metro development bundle reported three `Require cycle` warnings from
the generated simulation modules:

- `world.js -> economy/validation.js -> economy/trade.js -> world.js`
- `world.js -> economy/validation.js -> economy/trade.js -> economy/monetary.js -> world.js`
- `world.js -> economy/military.js -> world.js`

`world.ts` must validate every subsystem before a tick and before save encoding.
The runtime phase modules (`trade.ts`, `monetary.ts`, `military.ts`) also called
`assertValidWorld*` after staging their work. Because `validation.ts` imported
those phase modules for their state validators, the whole-world validator and the
phase modules imported each other. The cycles were structural but still created
partially initialized exports and Metro warnings.

### Decision

1. `world.ts` remains the authoritative `World` state definition and the
   whole-world validator. It is the top of the validation hierarchy.
2. State construction, lookups, and structural validation live in new lower-level
   modules:
   - `economy/tradeState.ts` — trade policy/state creation and trade validators;
   - `economy/monetaryState.ts` — price-index/monetary state factories, shared
     valuation/account helpers, and monetary validators;
   - `economy/militaryState.ts` — military state construction, stable lookups,
     and the military validator.
   These modules import `World` only with `import type`; they never import world
   initialization at runtime.
3. `world.ts` and `economy/validation.ts` import the low-level state/validation
   modules. They no longer import `trade.ts`, `monetary.ts`, or `military.ts`.
4. The runtime phase modules keep their self-validation contract by importing
   `assertValidWorld*` from `world.ts`, but `world.ts` no longer imports them, so
   the runtime graph is acyclic. They re-export their state surface so the public
   `@century/sim` API, scenario construction and existing tests do not change.
5. A reusable checker, `scripts/m11e/check-sim-dependencies.mjs` (root script
   `pnpm check:sim-deps`), builds the TypeScript runtime graph (ignoring
   type-only imports) and the generated JavaScript graph, and fails on any
   strongly connected component. Jest regressions pin the hierarchy.

### Consequences

- All three Metro require cycles are gone, along with the previously unreported
  `world -> profit -> validation -> trade -> ...` component.
- Whole-world validation, phase validation timing, economic/monetary/military
  behavior, determinism, serialization, and save schemas are unchanged.
- Type-only imports are exactly the erased interface bridge; no logic was
  duplicated and no generic utility module was introduced.
- `pnpm ci` now runs `check:sim-deps` after the library build.

## D-102 — M11e: platform-specific WorldMap SVG selection and geometry validation

**Status:** implemented (M11e).

### Context

The development console reported repeated React DOM errors originating at
`react-native-svg/lib/module/web/WebShape.js` and the polity `Path` render in
`WorldMap.tsx`:

`Unknown event handler property `onStartShouldSetResponder`. It will be ignored.`

and the same for `onResponderTerminationRequest`, `onResponderGrant`,
`onResponderMove`, `onResponderRelease`, `onResponderTerminate` and `onPressIn`.
`WebShape` treats any `onPress`/`onPressIn` prop as a React Native touchable and
injects responder props into the DOM SVG node. React DOM rejects them. The
production export suppresses these development warnings, which is why the
exported build appeared clean; the defect is still real in development and on
any future stricter DOM runtime.

### Decision

1. Selection props are platform-specific. On web, polity fills and small-state
   markers use `onClickCapture`; `WebShape.prepare` only rewrites `onClick`, so
   the capture handler reaches the DOM intact and no touchable mixin is engaged.
   Native keeps `onPress`/`onPressIn`.
2. Web hover continues to use the container's `onPointerMove`; the native-only
   `onPressIn` is not needed on web.
3. A drag that ends in a browser click does not select a country: the pan
   responder records movement and suppresses the following click briefly.
4. Map projection, ring decoding, path building and validation move to the pure
   `worldMapGeometry.ts`. Malformed rings (odd coordinates, non-finite values,
   fewer than three points, missing/empty rings) throw a tagged `RangeError`
   instead of being silently skipped. A polity with no rings at all has the
   explicit marker fallback; it is never discarded.
5. Regression tests decode all 133 polities, build the complete 267-path/17-marker
   render plan, cover invalid geometries and the empty fallback, and pin the
   absence of runtime `onPress`/`onPressIn` on web SVG shapes.

### Consequences

- The repeated WebShape console errors and the three require-cycle warnings are
  gone from the development console; only the expected React DevTools information
  message remains.
- Country selection, hover, zoom and panning are unchanged in the browser; native
  touch behavior is unchanged.
- Map data, historical borders, simulation state, saves, determinism and the
  public API are unchanged.

## D-103 — M12a: permanent provinces replace geographical regions

**Status:** implemented (M12a).

### Context

The M11 world had one map polygon per 1900 polity/territory and one simulation
`Region` per catalog entry. The simulation `Region` is a population/economy
accounting unit: it owns pop blocks, buildings, a market and a controller. It was
never a separately modelled geographical subdivision, but because one map polygon
corresponded to one region, the two concepts were easy to confuse. M12a requires
that the sole geographical subdivision beneath a polity be a permanent province
whose ownership can change without rebuilding country polygons.

### Decision

1. **Province is the only geographical layer.** A committed province bundle
   (`packages/data/data/map/provinces_1900.json`) carries stable ids, historical
   names, 1900 owners, administrative metadata, provenance, confidence and
   simplified geometry. There is no geographical region entity in the province
   hierarchy and no Region → Province nesting.
2. **The simulation Region is preserved, not repurposed.** It remains the
   population/economy accounting unit with the existing serialized field name
   `regions` for save compatibility. Explicit aliases `SimulationRegion`,
   `SimulationRegionInitialDef`, `SimulationRegionMetaDef` and
   `SimulationRegionSnapshot` document the distinction. Provinces reference the
   simulation region that currently contains their population
   (`simulationRegionId`). Province geometry is never derived from it.
3. **Mutable political state is separate from permanent geography.**
   `World.provinces` stores legal ownership, effective control, the control date,
   formal claims and special status for every province. Mutations
   (`transferProvinceOwnership`, `setProvinceControl`, claims, special status)
   never touch geometry, names or ids. Occupation changes only the controller.
4. **Country borders are derived from current provincial ownership.** Province
   boundaries are the province fill outlines themselves: each province path
   carries a quiet solid white hairline, so shared edges are double-stroked at
   identical coordinates and stay continuous and aligned with the fills. At the
   1900 start state the continuous polity outline is the exact boundary of its
   province union and is drawn as the country border at the established
   non-European weight (about 1.25 screen px); it is deliberately not a heavy
   outline. Once a province changes hands, the changed polity's static outline is
   suppressed so a stale 1900 border cannot survive, and the border is derived
   from current province ownership.
5. **Geometry source.** Natural Earth 1:10m admin-1 units are internal blocks
   only; every province is clipped to the authored M11c 1900 polity polygon.
   Later borders that cut a 1900 unit are reconstructed as separate provinces
   (East Prussia north/south, Silesia thirds, West Prussia/Posen, Schleswig,
   Alsace/Lorraine, Galicia/Bukovina, Transylvania/Banat, Ireland's 26 + 6
   counties, and the Baltic/Ukrainian/Belarusian governorate groups).
6. **Stable ids.** Province ids are assigned deterministically as
   `ownerPolityId * 1000 + order` in the authored plan and never renumbered.
   Future polity ids start at 1000 and never collide with the 0..165 catalog ids.
7. **Future successors are a registry, not 1900 countries.** Poland,
   Czechoslovakia, Yugoslavia, Finland, Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania, East/West
   Germany, Ireland, Austria/Hungary successors, Danzig, Albania, Ukraine,
   Belarus and Moldova are registered with activation dates and remain absent
   from the 1900 selectable-country list.
8. **Schema and version.** `World` schema 26 → 27 (adds `World.provinces`),
   simulation version 0.27.0 → 0.28.0, worker protocol 14 → 15 (adds the
   `provinces` snapshot array). The save envelope and round-trip format are
   unchanged; the project keeps its existing strict same-version save policy.
   The data content version stays `m3-economy-v12` because existing definition
   and scenario meanings are unchanged and the province content is additive.
9. **No population/economic redistribution.** Existing simulation regions,
   populations and buildings are untouched so determinism and starting
   conditions are preserved. Province-level simulation is explicitly deferred.

### Consequences

- Austria-Hungary, Germany, Russia and the United Kingdom can lose, gain and
  exchange territory through province ownership without changing any polygon.
- Reference snapshots for 1900, 1918–1920, 1945, 1949 and 1990–1991 are
  reproducible via transfers; the tests demonstrate the key sequences.
- The old geographical dependency is gone: map rendering, hit-testing,
  selection and country composition use provinces. The simulation region only
  supplies population/economy totals to the UI.
- Known approximation: modern admin-1 units are proxies for historical internal
  boundaries, and a handful of authored clip lines approximate borders. These are
  labelled low/medium confidence in the inventory and in
  `docs/EUROPE_PROVINCIALIZATION.md`; they are not presented as surveyed lines.
- Province geometry adds about 1 MiB to the committed offline bundle and about
  1,300 paths to the map. The map remains a single offline SVG render.

## D-104 — M12b: Asian provinces extend the same permanent-province model

### Decision

1. **One model, two bundles.** Asia reuses the M12a province model and rendering
   contracts exactly. There is still one geographical subdivision, `Province`;
   no Polity → Region → Province hierarchy and no new simulation model were
   introduced. Europe and Asia are separate deterministic build artifacts
   (`provinces_1900.json`, `provinces_asia_1900.json`) combined by one loader in
   `packages/data/src/provinces.ts` (simulation definitions) and
   `packages/app/src/provinceMapBundle.ts` (rendering, merged borders and future
   polities).
2. **Disjoint ids and keys.** Asia province ids start at 100001, above the M12a
   Europe block (maximum 66001). Every Asia key is prefixed `as-`. The build and
   both loaders reject an id/key collision instead of silently replacing a
   province. Europe ids and keys are unchanged by M12b.
3. **Shared geometry pipeline.** The M12a build helpers were extracted into
   `scripts/m12/province-build-lib.mjs`. Both builds use the same clipping,
   simplification, cross-owner trimming and border-extraction code. Re-running
   the Europe build after the extraction reproduces the committed European
   bundle exactly (570 provinces, 1604 border pairs, 142610 segments, 67
   multi-neighbour segments, 38 small overlaps).
4. **Cross-bundle overlap priority.** The Asia build seeds the committed European
   provinces as earlier, higher-priority geometry. An Asian province is clipped
   by a European province of a different owner so it cannot paint a
   foreign-coloured sliver across an already reviewed frontier; same-owner
   cross-bundle overlap is not trimmed because it cannot produce a foreign
   enclave.
5. **Future polity range.** Asia future successor polities use ids 1100–1199.
   They never collide with the M12a Europe range 1000–1016 and are never
   selectable in 1900. The registry includes the Ottoman successor states, the
   mandates, the post-colonial states of South and Southeast Asia, the Chinese,
   Korean and Mongolian successors, and the 1991 Central Asian and Caucasian
   republics.
6. **Successor-border splits.** A province is the permanent geographical unit, but
   one 1900 administrative area may be represented by more than one permanent
   province when a later successor frontier runs through it. Asia therefore
   splits Indian/West Punjab; Indian, Azad and Aksai Chin Kashmir; Lebanon/Syrian
   Coast; Aleppo/Urfa; Hatay; Jerusalem/Palestine; South/North Gangwon;
   Batum/Artvin, Erivan/Iğdır and Kars; Kazakh/Uzbek Syr-Darya; Fergana/Osh;
   Samarkand/Khojand; Pamir/Wakhan; and Khiva/Tashauz. Manchuria additionally has
   Rehe and Xing'an as separate Qing/Manchukuo-scale provinces, approximated
   through modern Hebei, Liaoning and Inner Mongolia. The split lines follow
   modern first-level units or the 1900 M11c frontier, are labelled low
   confidence, and are never dynamic geometry surgery: each split is a data
   definition with its own stable id. This is what makes the 1947 Radcliffe line,
   the 1949 Israel/Palestine and Kashmir ceasefire lines, the 38th-parallel
   Korean division, the 1921 Turkish frontier, the 1932-1933 Manchukuo extent and
   the 1991 Central Asian and Caucasian borders representable without moving a
   polygon.
7. **Mutable ownership, derived borders.** Asian provinces use the same
   `World.provinces` ownership/control/claims/special-status state and the same
   snapshot contract as Europe. Country borders are derived from current
   province ownership; the 1900 polity outline is used only while the province
   union still equals that outline.
8. **Schema and save policy.** `World` schema stays 27, the save envelope and
   worker protocol are unchanged, and the data content version stays
   `m3-economy-v12`: M12b is additive content, not a new state shape. An
   M12a-era save therefore loads under the existing strict same-version policy
   but contains only the 570 European ownership records; its Asian provinces
   have no mutable record until a new campaign is created.

### Consequences

- The 1900 world now has 809 permanent provinces (570 Europe + 239 Asia) in one
  combined catalogue with 2237 border pairs and one future-polity registry.
- The 1900, 1918–1920, 1932–1939, 1945, 1947–1949, 1988 and 1990–1991 Asian
  configurations are reproducible through ordinary ownership transfers,
  effective-control changes, claims and special statuses; no polygon changes.
- The map adds about 0.8 MiB of Asia geometry and about 239 province paths. The
  map remains one offline SVG render with the M12a visual hierarchy: solid white
  province hairlines, thin near-black country borders and no dashed province
  lines.
- Known approximations: the M11c Russian/Afghan/Chinese Pamir and Uriankhai
  frontiers, Siamese 1907/1909 losses, Gwadar, the Dodecanese, Kinmen, the
  successor-border split lines, and the use of modern admin-1 units as
  historical proxies. They are labelled low or medium confidence in
  `docs/ASIA_PROVINCIALIZATION.md` §9 and in the bundle.
- Coverage residual is the sum of many small coastal/frontier slivers. It stays
  under the correct 1900 owner's polity fill, so it cannot appear as a
  foreign-coloured enclave; the audit records every unassigned unit and every
  cross-frontier code assignment.


## D-105 — M12c: African provinces complete the three-bundle model

**Status:** implemented (M12c).

### Decision

1. **One model, three bundles.** Africa reuses the M12a/M12b province model and
   rendering contracts exactly. There is still one geographical subdivision,
   `Province`; no Polity → Region → Province hierarchy and no new simulation
   model were introduced. Europe, Asia and Africa are separate deterministic
   build artifacts (`provinces_1900.json`, `provinces_asia_1900.json`,
   `provinces_africa_1900.json`) combined by one loader in
   `packages/data/src/provinces.ts` (simulation definitions) and
   `packages/app/src/provinceMapBundle.ts` (rendering, merged borders and future
   polities).
2. **Disjoint ids and keys.** Africa province ids start at 200001, above the M12a
   Europe block (maximum 66001) and the M12b Asia block (100001–100239). Every
   Africa key is prefixed `af-`. The build and both loaders reject an id/key
   collision. Europe and Asia ids and keys are unchanged by M12c.
3. **Shared geometry pipeline.** `scripts/m12/province-build-lib.mjs` is reused
   unchanged. The Africa build seeds **both** committed bundles (Europe first,
   then Asia) as higher-priority cross-owner geometry, so an African province
   cannot paint a foreign-coloured sliver across an already reviewed frontier.
4. **Coverage scope is per shared feature.** Africa shares several map features
   with Europe and Asia. The build measures coverage only over the African part:
   Ottoman Libya within the Ottoman Empire, Réunion and Mayotte within France, the
   African islands within the Portuguese Atlantic and British Indian/South
   Atlantic features, Morocco north of Western Sahara and the Cape without the
   Prince Edward Islands. Coverage clips are audit boundaries, not political
   borders.
5. **Display-only cross-feature seam borders.** Shared border extraction is
   exact only between two provinces of the same 1900 map feature. Frontiers
   between two features (Eritrea/Ethiopia, British/Italian Somaliland,
   Kenya/Tanganyika, Cabinda/Congo, Nigeria/Kamerun, ...) are clipped by two
   independently authored polity polygons and can be several tenths of a degree
   apart, so the derived national border would be missing there after a transfer.
   The build adds deterministic, display-only seams in two passes: a
   province-outline pass (nearest point on the other outline, midpoint, 0.5°
   tolerance, point-to-segment so straight frontiers work, with land, polity and
   third-province filters) and a polity-frontier pass for feature pairs the first
   pass cannot connect (midline between the two 1900 polity outlines, 0.4°
   tolerance, plus a forced Algeria/Mauritania frontier). Neither changes a
   province polygon, id or simulation state; both follow the M11c map's existing
   display-only seam-bridge precedent. **183** seam pairs are committed (10 of
   them polity-frontier seams) and listed in the bundle audit and in
   `docs/AFRICA_PROVINCIALIZATION.md` §3.1. The audit proves that every modern
   African land border has a province edge except three pairs that are not
   borders: the Botswana/Zambia quadripoint, the undelimited Kenya/South Sudan
   Ilemi Triangle (unassigned in the M11c map), and Djibouti/Somalia, which is
   mediated by Somaliland.
6. **Future polity range.** Africa future successor polities use ids 1200–1256.
   They never collide with the M12a Europe range (1000–1016) or the M12b Asia
   range (1100–1144) and are never selectable in 1900. They cover every sovereign
   African state that appeared between 1900 and 2000, including Tanganyika (1961),
   Zanzibar (1963) and Tanzania (1964), and the Rhodesian sequence (Rhodesia
   (1965), Zimbabwe Rhodesia (1979), Zimbabwe (1980)).
7. **Successor-border splits.** A province is the permanent geographical unit, but
   one 1900 administrative area is represented by more than one permanent
   province wherever a later successor frontier runs through it: Sudan/South
   Sudan; German East Africa into Tanganyika, Rwanda and Burundi; Kamerun into its
   French and British portions; Togoland likewise; Nigeria into Lagos, Southern
   and Northern Nigeria (plus the separate Sokoto emirates); French West Africa
   and French Equatorial Africa into their modern successor groups; Northern and
   Southern Rhodesia and Nyasaland; Bechuanaland, Basutoland and Swaziland; the
   Cape Colony and Walvis Bay; Libya into Tripolitania, Cyrenaica and Fezzan;
   Morocco into its Spanish and French zones; Portuguese Guinea, Cape Verde, São
   Tomé and Príncipe. Every pair shares a continuous province edge in the bundle,
   proved by the M12c test suite. The split lines follow modern first-level units
   or an authored historical line; none moves a polygon.
8. **Mutable ownership, derived borders.** African provinces use the same
   `World.provinces` ownership/control/claims/special-status state and the same
   snapshot contract. Sudan starts as an explicit `condominium`; future mandates
   and protectorates are ordinary control/status changes. Country borders are
   derived from current province ownership.
9. **Schema and save policy.** `World` schema stays 27, the save envelope and the
   worker protocol are unchanged, and the data content version is unchanged: M12c
   is additive content, not a new state shape. An M12a-era save (570 European
   records) or an M12b-era save (809 European and Asian records) still loads under
   the strict same-version policy but has no mutable record for its African
   provinces until a new campaign is created.
10. **South Sudan activation is clamped to the campaign boundary.** The
    simulation calendar covers 1900-01-01 to 2000-01-01. South Sudan's historical
    independence (9 July 2011) is outside that window, so the future-polity
    registry activation is set to 2000-01-01 and its note records the real date.
    The southern Sudanese provinces are permanently separate from 1900, so the
    transfer itself remains a single ordinary ownership change.

### Consequences

- The 1900 world now has **1069** permanent provinces (570 Europe + 239 Asia +
  260 Africa) in one combined catalogue with one future-polity registry
  (17 + 45 + 57 = 119 future polities) and 3075 border pairs
  (1604 + 633 + 838). 16 polities own African provinces.
- The 1900, 1903–1916, 1910, 1911–1912, 1919–1922, 1936–1941, 1956–1968,
  1975–1977, 1980–1994 and 2011 African configurations are reproducible through
  ordinary ownership transfers, effective-control changes, claims and special
  statuses; no polygon changes.
- The map adds about 0.54 MiB of African geometry and about 260 province paths.
  It remains one offline SVG render with the established visual hierarchy: solid
  white province hairlines, thin near-black country borders at 1.25 screen px and
  no dashed province lines.
- Known approximations: the 26th-parallel Saguia el-Hamra/Río de Oro line, the
  Tarfaya strip, the Spanish presidios (not exposed by the M11c map), the
  French/Spanish Moroccan zone line, the Libyan internal boundaries, the
  British/French Togoland and Cameroons partition lines, the coarser Cape/Natal/
  Orange Free State and Bechuanaland province granularity, and the use of modern
  admin-1 units as historical proxies. They are labelled low or medium confidence
  in `docs/AFRICA_PROVINCIALIZATION.md` §9 and in the bundle.
- Coverage residual is the sum of many small coastal, lake and frontier slivers.
  It stays under the correct 1900 owner's polity fill, so it cannot appear as a
  foreign-coloured enclave; the audit records every unassigned unit, every
  cross-frontier code assignment and every seam border.

## D-106 — M12d: American provinces complete the four-bundle model

**Status:** implemented (M12d); the polity registry is extended by D-107.

### Context

M12a Europe, M12b Asia and M12c Africa left the Americas as the last 1900
theatre without permanent provinces. The M11c map already carried separate
features for the United States, Canada, Newfoundland, Mexico, the Central
American republics, Colombia (including Panama), Venezuela, Ecuador, Peru,
Bolivia, Chile, Argentina, Uruguay, Paraguay, Brazil, Haiti, the Dominican
Republic, Cuba, Puerto Rico, the British, French, Dutch and Danish Caribbean,
Suriname, Greenland and the British South Atlantic. The task required the same
permanent-province model, mutable ownership and reconstructed country borders,
without a Polity → Region → Province hierarchy.

### Decision

1. **One model, four bundles.** The Americas reuse the M12a/M12b/M12c province
   model and rendering contracts exactly. There is still one geographical
   subdivision, `Province`; no Polity → Region → Province hierarchy and no new
   simulation model were introduced. Europe, Asia, Africa and the Americas are
   separate deterministic build artifacts (`provinces_1900.json`,
   `provinces_asia_1900.json`, `provinces_africa_1900.json`,
   `provinces_americas_1900.json`) combined by one loader in
   `packages/data/src/provinces.ts` (simulation definitions) and
   `packages/app/src/provinceMapBundle.ts` (rendering, merged borders and future
   polities).
2. **Disjoint ids and keys.** Americas province ids start at 300001, above the
   M12a Europe block (maximum 66001), the M12b Asia block (100001–100239) and the
   M12c Africa block (200001–200260). Every Americas key is prefixed `am-`. The
   build and both loaders reject an id/key collision. Europe, Asia and Africa
   ids and keys are unchanged by M12d.
3. **Shared geometry pipeline.** `scripts/m12/province-build-lib.mjs` is reused.
   The Americas build seeds **all three** committed bundles (Europe first, then
   Asia, then Africa) as higher-priority cross-owner geometry, so an American
   province cannot paint a foreign-coloured sliver across an already reviewed
   frontier.
4. **Coverage scope is per shared feature.** The Americas share the French
   feature with Europe/Africa and the British South Atlantic feature with
   Africa/Asia/Oceania. Coverage is measured only over French Guiana and the
   French Antilles (two disjoint rings, which is why the shared pipeline now
   accepts a multi-ring coverage scope) and over the Falklands, South Georgia and
   the South Sandwich Islands. Coverage clips are audit boundaries, not political
   borders. BWI, FCA, DGC, DWI, SUR, GRL, PRI and every sovereign American
   feature are wholly American and need no clip.
5. **Future polity range.** Americas future successor polities use ids 1300–1399.
   They never collide with the M12a Europe range (1000–1016), the M12b Asia range
   (1100–1144) or the M12c Africa range (1200–1256), and are never selectable in
   1900. The registry holds Panama (1903), the British Caribbean independences,
   Belize, Guyana, Suriname, and the optional Netherlands Antilles (1954) and
   Aruba (1986) status polities.
6. **Newfoundland is the one 1900-active registry entry.** Newfoundland was a
   self-governing Dominion on 1 January 1900 but is absent from the 67-polity
   M11c selectable catalog. It is registered as a non-selectable 1900 polity
   (`kind: "state"`, `activeFrom: 1900-01-01`, `activeTo: 1949-03-31`) so its
   permanent provinces are distinct from Canada and the 1949 union is an ordinary
   ownership transfer. The data loader accepts an explicit `kind` and
   `selectable1900` on a future-polity record; the defaults remain `future-state`
   and `false`, so the earlier bundles are unchanged.
7. **Successor-border splits.** A province is the permanent geographical unit, but
   one 1900 administrative area is represented by more than one permanent
   province wherever a later successor frontier runs through it: Colombia/Panama;
   Canada/Newfoundland; the three Danish West Indies islands; every British
   Caribbean colony that became a separate state; the French and Dutch island
   groups; Acre and Tacna. The split lines are modern first-level units or the
   1900 M11c frontier, never a crude rectangular correction box. Every pair shares
   a continuous province edge in the bundle, proved by the M12d test.
8. **Mutable ownership, derived borders.** American provinces use the same
   `World.provinces` ownership/control/claims/special-status state and the same
   snapshot contract. Cuba and Puerto Rico are United States-owned in 1900
   (Cuba transfers to `CUB` in 1902); Panama is Colombian until 1903; the Danish
   West Indies are Danish until 1917; Newfoundland is its own registry polity
   until 1949. Country borders are derived from current province ownership; the
   1900 polity outline is used only while the province union still equals it.
9. **Display-only cross-feature seams.** As in M12c, shared border extraction is
   exact only within one 1900 feature. The build adds deterministic seams in a
   province-outline pass (point-to-segment, 0.5° tolerance, land/polity/third-
   province filters) and a polity-frontier pass (midline within 0.4°). The
   committed bundle has 178 seam pairs. The M12d test asserts that all 38 modern
   sovereign American land borders are backed by an edge. The only American land
   border outside the sovereign audit is the non-sovereign Saint-Martin/Sint
   Maarten line; both halves are separate provinces, but the M11c land mask does
   not resolve the island.
10. **Optional episodes.** The West Indies Federation is not an ownership layer:
    it was a federation of Crown colonies and never held provincial ownership;
    the islands are separate permanent provinces so the episode can be added
    later. The Netherlands Antilles and Aruba are registry polities with ordinary
    transfers. The Panama Canal Zone is not a separate province: it was a lease,
    not a sovereignty transfer, and a five-mile strip would require a crude
    authored rectangle; United States control and the 1979 reversion are
    representable as controller/special-status changes on the Panama province.
11. **Schema and save policy.** `World` schema stays 27, the save envelope and
    worker protocol are unchanged, and the data content version is unchanged:
    M12d is additive content, not a new state shape. An M12a-era save (570
    European records), an M12b-era save (809 European and Asian records) or an
    M12c-era save (1069 European, Asian and African records) still loads under the
    strict same-version policy but has no mutable record for its American
    provinces until a new campaign is created.
12. **Twentieth-century state and border audit.** Every sovereign American state
    that appears between 1900 and 2000 is registered as a 1900 catalog polity, a
    future polity, or both (36 states, 0 missing). The test and the standalone
    audit enumerate 38 modern sovereign land borders and prove each is backed by
    a shared or seam province edge.

### Consequences

- The 1900 world now has **1538** permanent provinces (570 Europe + 239 Asia +
  260 Africa + 469 Americas) in one combined catalogue with one future-polity
  registry (17 + 45 + 57 + 17 = 136 future polities) and **4444** border pairs
  (1604 + 633 + 838 + 1369). 25 polities own American provinces.
- The 1900, 1902, 1903, 1917, 1929, 1949, 1954, 1962–1983, 1975, 1979 and 1986
  American configurations are reproducible through ordinary ownership
  transfers, effective-control changes, claims and special statuses; no polygon
  changes.
- The map adds about 1.1 MiB of American geometry and 469 province paths. It
  remains one offline SVG render with the established visual hierarchy: solid
  white province hairlines, thin near-black country borders at 1.25 screen px and
  no dashed province lines.
- Known approximations: modern admin-1 units as 1900 proxies; grouped Oklahoma
  Territory/Indian Territory; Alberta/Saskatchewan as Northwest Territories
  districts; a single 1900 Baja California; grouped Brazilian territories and
  the modern Federal District inside Goiás; Carriacou grouped with Grenada;
  San Andrés attached to Bolívar; Bonaire/Saba/Sint Eustatius not exposed; the
  Saint-Martin/Sint Maarten seam; the Canal Zone control approximation; and the
  Newfoundland registry exception. They are labelled low or medium confidence in
  `docs/AMERICAS_PROVINCIALIZATION.md` §9 and in the bundle.
- Coverage residual is the sum of many small coastal, river and frontier slivers.
  It stays under the correct 1900 owner's polity fill, so it cannot appear as a
  foreign-coloured enclave; the audit records every unassigned unit, every
  cross-frontier code assignment and every seam border.
- D-107 extends the polity registry with 30 historical/breakaway entries (47
  Americas registry entries, 166 combined). It changes no province, border or
  save count; it only adds registry metadata.

## D-107 — M12d addendum: historical and breakaway polities in the Americas registry

**Status:** implemented (M12d addendum).

### Context

The M12d sovereign-state audit covered every American sovereign state that
appeared between 1900 and 2000, but the game may also want to represent
historical breakaways and alternate-history states. Texas, the Riograndense
Republic and the California Republic are the clearest examples; the same
question applies to the other declared American breakaway republics, the
short-lived twentieth-century states (Acre, Anguilla, the West Indies
Federation) and the predecessor unions that could be restored in an
alternate-history scenario.

### Decision

1. **A new registry kind, not a new geography layer.** `PolityDef.kind` gains
   `"historical"`. A historical entry is registry metadata only: it is never a
   1900 owner, never selectable, and never owns a province at the start. The
   province model, ids, geometry, rendering and save format are unchanged.
2. **30 historical and breakaway entries.** The Americas registry now holds 47
   entries: the 17 post-1900 successors already delivered by M12d plus 30
   historical/breakaway entries with ids **1317–1346**:
   - **United States breakaways / alternatives:** Texas (TEX), California
     (CAL), Deseret (DES), Confederate States of America (CSA), West Florida
     (WFL), the Floridas (FLR), Indian Stream (INS) and Madawaska (MDW).
   - **Latin American breakaways:** Riograndense Republic (RGR), Juliana
     Republic (JUL), Republic of the Rio Grande (RRG), Yucatán (YUC), Acre
     (ACR), Counani (CNU), Sonora (SON), Lower California (LBC), the Equator
     (EQT), Bahia (BAH), Pernambuco (PEM) and Entre Ríos (ERY).
   - **Canada:** Republic of Canada (RCN) and Republic of Lower Canada (RLC).
   - **Short-lived twentieth-century states:** Republic of Acre (ACR, real
     start 1899, end 1903), Republic of Anguilla (AIA, 1967–1969) and the West
     Indies Federation (WIF, 1958–1962).
   - **Predecessor unions:** Gran Colombia (GCO), United Provinces of the Río
     de la Plata (UPR), Argentine Confederation (ACF), State of Buenos Aires
     (SBA) and the Peru-Bolivian Confederation (PBC).
3. **Calendar clamp.** A pre-1900 state cannot use its real activation date
   inside the 1900–2000 calendar. Its `activeFrom`/`activeTo` are clamped to
   1900-01-01 (the documented last resort), or to the real end when that falls
   inside the window (Acre ends 1903, Anguilla 1969, the West Indies Federation
   1962). The real dates are recorded in the entry notes; no other data is
   altered.
4. **Historical entries are usable by scenario transfers.** A province can be
   transferred to a historical polity by the ordinary ownership API. The M12d
   test proves alternate-history transfers of Texas, California, the
   Riograndense Republic and Yucatán, without changing any province id or
   geometry.
5. **Semihistorical entries are labelled.** Counani, West Florida, the Floridas,
   Indian Stream, Madawaska, the Canadian rebellions, Sonora/Lower California
   and the Brazilian provincial republics are low or medium confidence; they are
   included because the user asked for semihistorical states, not presented as
   uncontested sovereign facts.
6. **Schema and save policy.** `World` schema stays 27, the save envelope and
   worker protocol are unchanged, and no simulation country is added. The
   registry remains static scenario content; old saves are unaffected.

### Consequences

- Americas registry entries: **47** (17 successors + 30 historical/breakaway).
  Combined registry: **166** entries (17 Europe + 45 Asia + 57 Africa + 47
  Americas). Combined catalogue remains **1538** provinces and **4444** border
  pairs; historical polities own nothing at the 1900 start.
- The M12d suite has **35 tests**, including a historical-registry inventory and
  an alternate-history transfer proof. The standalone audit prints **30
  historical/breakaway entries, 0 missing** in addition to the 36-state and
  38-border checks.
- The registry now answers "what historical American states could appear?" for
  the declared breakaways the user named and for the documented short-lived and
  predecessor states. It does not add every filibuster or every proposed
  subdivision; entries are limited to states with a declared government and a
  documented name. That boundary is recorded in
  `docs/AMERICAS_PROVINCIALIZATION.md` §4.2.

## D-108 — M12e: Oceanian provinces and global land-gap closure

**Status:** implemented (M12e).

### Decision

1. **One model, five bundles.** Oceania reuses the M12a-M12d province model and
   rendering contracts exactly. There is still one geographical subdivision,
   `Province`; no Polity -> Region -> Province hierarchy and no new simulation
   model were introduced. Europe, Asia, Africa, the Americas and Oceania are
   separate deterministic build artifacts (`provinces_1900.json`,
   `provinces_asia_1900.json`, `provinces_africa_1900.json`,
   `provinces_americas_1900.json`, `provinces_oceania_1900.json`) combined by one
   loader in `packages/data/src/provinces.ts` (simulation definitions) and
   `packages/app/src/provinceMapBundle.ts` (rendering, merged borders and future
   polities).
2. **Disjoint ids and keys.** Oceania province ids start at 400001, above the
   M12a Europe block (maximum 66001), the M12b Asia block (100001-100239), the
   M12c Africa block (200001-200260) and the M12d Americas block
   (300001-300469). Every Oceania key is prefixed `oc-`. The build and both
   loaders reject an id/key collision. The block is partitioned deterministically:
   400001-404999 authored provinces, 405001-405999 one residual province per 1900
   map feature, 406001-406999 the automatic land-ring closure.
3. **Shared geometry pipeline.** `scripts/m12/province-build-lib.mjs` is reused.
   The Oceania build seeds **all four** committed bundles (Europe, Asia, Africa,
   then the Americas) as higher-priority cross-owner geometry, so an Oceanian
   province cannot paint a foreign-coloured sliver across an already reviewed
   frontier. The library gained generic `detached` and gap-province support rather
   than forking the pipeline: `detached` provinces use the modern admin-1 island
   coastline without intersecting the coarse 1900 feature; `feature-residual` and
   `land-ring` gap provinces are computed as the difference between the 1900
   feature (or the M11c land ring) and every existing province of every bundle.
4. **Coverage scope is per shared feature.** The build measures coverage only over
   the Oceanian part of a shared feature: the British Pacific feature is scoped to
   the two Pacific boxes (its Indian Ocean islands are Africa/Asia provinces); the
   British South Atlantic feature to Pitcairn and Chagos; Chile to Easter Island
   and Juan Fernandez; Mexico to Revillagigedo; the United States to the Pacific
   remote islands and Navassa (empty in the 1900 feature, so the residual is zero
   rather than the whole United States); the United Kingdom to the Isle of Man,
   Norway to Svalbard and Austria-Hungary to Krk/Cres/Losinj. Coverage clips are
   audit boundaries, not political borders.
5. **Australia.** The six 1900 colonies are permanent provinces. The Northern
   Territory (part of South Australia), the Federal Capital Territory and Jervis
   Bay (part of New South Wales) are separate permanent provinces so the 1911
   transfers are ordinary ownership changes. The 1901 federation transfers the ten
   Australian provinces to the future Commonwealth of Australia (`AUS`, 1400); the
   tag is shared with the 1900 catalog polity so the palette colour is unchanged,
   following the M12d Newfoundland precedent that a future tag may equal a 1900
   tag. Macquarie Island is a detached Australian province; Lord Howe Island is a
   second polygon part of the New South Wales admin-1 unit and therefore part of
   the New South Wales province.
6. **New Zealand.** The modern regional-council areas are the permanent proxy for
   the 1900 provincial-district subdivision (the provinces were abolished in 1876).
   Stewart Island, the Chatham Islands, the Kermadecs, the Three Kings, the
   Auckland Islands, Campbell Island, the Antipodes and The Snares are separate
   provinces. The 1907 dominion is a status change, not an ownership change; `NZD`
   is registered for the state audit but owns no province. Tokelau is owned by the
   United Kingdom in 1900 (British protectorate) even though the M11c New Zealand
   feature contains the atolls because of the modern association.
7. **Papua New Guinea.** German New Guinea and British New Guinea (Papua) are
   separate 1900 province groups. The M11c `german-new-guinea-papua` patch assigns
   the southern first-level units to the British Pacific feature; the modern PNG
   province boundaries are the permanent provinces and the proxy for the 1885-86
   central-range line. The 1906 Australian administration, the 1914 occupation, the
   1920 League mandate, the 1949 union and 1975 independence are ordinary
   control/ownership changes. The only modern sovereign Oceanian land border is the
   141 E Papua New Guinea / Indonesia line, backed by a cross-bundle seam edge.
8. **Melanesia, Micronesia and Polynesia.** One province per colonial island group
   or modern first-level unit. Fiji uses its five colonial divisions; the Solomon
   Islands use the modern provincial units of the 1900 protectorate (the northern
   Solomons are German and part of German New Guinea); the New Hebrides are six
   `condominium` provinces owned by the United Kingdom with France as
   co-administrator; New Caledonia uses its three provinces. Nauru, the Marshall
   chains, the four Caroline groups, Palau, the four Northern Mariana units, Guam,
   the three Kiribati groups, the Ellice Islands, Tonga's five divisions, German
   Samoa, American Samoa, the two Cook Island groups, Niue, Tokelau, the five
   French Polynesian subdivisions, the two Wallis and Futuna provinces, Pitcairn,
   Easter Island and Juan Fernandez are permanent provinces with the required
   successor sequences.
9. **Remote islands and gaps.** Islands the coarse 1900 feature omits are detached
   provinces built from the modern admin-1 island (Pitcairn, Easter Island, Juan
   Fernandez, Revillagigedo, Wake/Midway/Johnston/Palmyra, Navassa, Tokelau,
   Kermadec, the New Zealand subantarctic islands, Heard/McDonald, Chagos). The
   M11c map does not expose Ashmore/Cartier, the Coral Sea islands, the Minerva
   Reefs, Christmas Island or the Cocos (Keeling) Islands (below the 0.02 square
   degree land-mask threshold and absent from the 1900 features and the admin-1
   layer); they are documented rather than silently dropped. Clipperton is required
   by the milestone but absent from every source, so it is a small authored island
   polygon (low confidence, coastline approximation). Svalbard is Norwegian with low
   confidence (terra nullius until 1920); Heard/McDonald are British with low
   confidence (unclaimed in 1900, annexed 1910, transferred to Australia 1947).
   No 1900 territory already covered by M12b/M12d is duplicated: the Philippines,
   Indonesia and Portuguese Timor stay Asian provinces, Hawaii stays
   `am-usa-hawaii`, and the Galapagos stay `am-ecu-galapagos`; M12e adds only the
   missing island rings of those shared features.
10. **Global coverage residual provinces.** The bundle also closes the remaining
    inland gaps of the earlier continents (Morocco's southern coast, French
    Equatorial Africa, the Ottoman and Greek residuals, Austria-Hungary, the British
    Isles, Russia, Romania, Germany, Algeria, China, British East Africa, Italian
    Somaliland, the Portuguese islands, Argentina, Canada, the Philippines and the
    Oceanian features). Each is computed as `feature - every province of every
    bundle`, owned by the feature's 1900 sovereign, and labelled `low` confidence
    coverage geometry. The German New Guinea residual is owned by the United
    Kingdom because the M11c feature retains pre-1885 coastal islets that belong to
    British Papua. A deterministic automatic land-ring closure is the final safety
    net; it emits no province in the committed build because the authored and
    residual partition already covers every non-Antarctic land ring.
11. **Display-only cross-feature and straight-frontier seams.** The M12c/M12d
    point-to-segment seam pass is reused for cross-feature frontiers and is also run
    for same-feature straight frontiers whose two admin-1 units carry different
    vertex densities (the 141 E NSW/SA line, the 26 S SA/NT line). The pass
    compares Oceanian provinces against the committed earlier bundles, so the
    Papua New Guinea / Indonesia border is a seam pair. Seams change no province
    polygon, id or simulation state.
12. **Antimeridian.** Province rings are stored unwrapped around the antimeridian
    (the Russian Empire feature runs to 190 E) and the renderer draws every ring at
    -360/0/+360 degrees. The global land audit and the independent test both test
    every sample point at the same three longitude offsets. Fiji, Kiribati and
    Tonga are the main antimeridian cases.
13. **Future polity range.** Oceania future successor polities use ids 1400-1499.
    They never collide with the M12a Europe range (1000-1016), the M12b Asia range
    (1100-1144), the M12c Africa range (1200-1256) or the M12d Americas range
    (1300-1346), and are never selectable in 1900. The registry holds the
    Commonwealth of Australia (1400), Papua New Guinea, Western Samoa, Nauru,
    Tuvalu, the Solomon Islands, Kiribati, Vanuatu, the Marshall Islands, the
    Federated States of Micronesia, Palau, the Cook Islands, Niue, Tokelau, Fiji,
    the Northern Mariana Islands and the Dominion of New Zealand, plus eight
    historical/breakaway entries (Republic of Hawaii, Kingdom of Tahiti, Kingdom of
    Fiji, Republic of the North Solomons, Tafea Nation, Republic of Vemerana,
    Republic of Minerva and the Principality of Hutt River). The Kingdom of Hawaii
    is registered by the M12d Americas bundle; a tag cannot repeat across the one
    combined registry, so the M12e registry adds the Republic of Hawaii instead.
14. **Global land audit.** `scripts/m12e/audit-global-land.mjs` decodes the M11c
    land mask, reconstructs land polygons with their holes, classifies Antarctica
    (maximum latitude below -60) and endorheic water holes (the Caspian), and tests
    every remaining land ring's representative vertices and a deterministic interior
    sample grid against every province with bbox-filtered point-in-polygon. A sample
    is assigned when it is inside a province or within the 0.075 degree coastline
    quantization band of a province outline (the band between the 0.04 degree land
    mask and the 0.02 degree province simplification; it is reported separately and
    is not an exemption for unassigned land). The audit writes
    `packages/data/data/map/global_land_audit.json`; the test re-derives the result
    independently. The committed result is 795 land rings, 93 Antarctic exceptions,
    1 water-hole exception, 701 non-Antarctic land rings all assigned, 0 unassigned
    area, 0 unassigned samples, 0 unassigned vertices, 133/133 map features with a
    province and 0 unassigned admin-1 units across the five bundles.
15. **Twentieth-century state and border audit.** The test and
    `scripts/m12e/audit-successors.mjs` enumerate every sovereign Oceanian state and
    self-governing entity between 1900 and 2000 (28 checked, 0 missing), the one
    modern sovereign land border (Papua New Guinea / Indonesia, backed by a province
    edge) and the eight historical/breakaway entries. The key transitions (1901
    federation, 1907 dominion, the German New Guinea and Samoa mandate sequences,
    the Japanese mandate and United States trust, the 1968-1980 independence wave
    and the 1986/1994 compacts) are driven as ordinary ownership/control changes in
    the M12e suite.
16. **Schema and save policy.** `World` schema stays 27, the save envelope and
    worker protocol are unchanged, and the data content version is unchanged: M12e
