1. **Sellable supply = opening stock + same-day output - funded input demand** (D-040
   counted opening stock plus same-day output). Producers buy inputs only from the opening
   stock, so a market must carry one day of input demand into the next day; D-040 counted
   that carry as supply, so every balanced market with intermediate goods read as a glut
   and drifted to the floor. The P2 world never showed it because it opened at the floor.
   **Deviation from the approved wording:** the designer approved "count opening stock
   only", on my description that households also buy only from the opening stock. The full
   suite then showed that households buy the day's output in Pass B (M5 sample: 3.53 M kg of
   meat bought against an opening stock of 18 k), so "opening stock only" read every final
   good as a permanent shortage, prices rose 5% a day and 40% of the M5 population went
   short of subsistence within a month (test `m5-feasibility`). The rule above keeps the
   approved intent (a balanced market holds its price) for both final and intermediate
   goods; it is recorded for the designer's review.
2. **Cash-neutral postings** compare the designated-cash residual with a tolerance relative
   to the posting's gross amount (its debits) rather than to the residual itself; at 1900
   scale one posting reaches 1e9-1e10 RUSD and the absolute 1e-6 bound was below double
   precision.
3. **Market lot pairing (D-133):** the lot-overrun checks (reservation vs lot in the
   pairing, Pass-B availability, final household consumption, producer input use) use a
   tolerance relative to the good's reserved total, since each reservation is a difference
   of cumulative interval ends; relative to the lot alone, a small lot in a 1e8-unit market
   overran by about 1e-8 and aborted the day.
4. **Trade dispatch** checks the in-transit consignment cap (512) per seller lot; the check
   per route and good let one route exceed the cap and abort the day.
5. **Public-enterprise support bound** rises from 1e9 to the general budget bound (1e15
   RUSD): 1900 treasuries reach several billion RUSD and the app offers up to the treasury.

### Consequences

- Trajectories change in every economic fixture (1); (2)-(4) only remove failures. Known
  answers are re-pinned with this decision as the reason.

## D-137 — Performance stage: measurement hooks, in-place phases, result detail, snapshot cache and save format 2

**Status:** implemented on `perf/10x` for review; the 10x acceptance target is not reached
(details in `docs/archive/completion-reports/PERF_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`). **Amends:** D-040 (save checksum), D-047
(period-close staging), D-116 (rollback), D-120 (save compatibility), D-123
(protocol/snapshot), D-133 (derived caches). **Acceptance basis:** the performance-stage
brief: host-path day mean and p50 at most 1/10 of the recorded baseline over 92+ days,
month start at most 3x the ordinary p50, save and load at most 1/10, new game at least 5x
faster, determinism, and identical outcomes where possible. The baseline is
`docs/perf/baseline.{json,txt}` (host day mean 2,356 / p50 2,190 ms, save 5,364 ms, load
5,107 ms, new game 990 ms, month starts 3.1-3.4x ordinary p50).

### Decision

1. **Phase observer (measurement only).** `TickContext.phaseObserver` is an optional
   callback the simulation invokes with a phase name at phase boundaries. The simulation
   never reads a clock and never reads anything back from the callback, so no outcome can
   depend on whether an observer is present. The benchmark (`perfBench`) and the
   profiler (`perfProfileDays`) use it to attribute wall time per phase. A test asserts
   the world hash is identical with and without an observer.
2. **`financeInPlace`.** Phase functions accept `financeInPlace` (default false). Direct
   callers keep the staged copy (`phaseFinance` clones the finance state, validates before
   publishing, and a failure leaves the world untouched). Inside the integrated day every
   phase writes finance in place: the day already owns atomicity through its own staging
   (staged tick) or the rollback runner (phase mode), and the cached finance index stays
   valid because the accounts array does not change. Every in-place phase records the
   opening designated-cash total before posting and checks conservation itself.
3. **In-day month-end validation consolidation.** On a civil month end the period close
   and the profit-distribution phase no longer repeat whole-finance and whole-summary
   validation that the same day's month-end whole-world validation performs before the
   day is published (`closeFinancePeriod`/`runProfitDistributionPhase` with
   `financeInPlace`). Direct callers and tests that invoke a phase on its own keep every
   check. The published world is still validated exactly once, after the close and the
   profit allocation, with `allowUnadvancedClose` false.
4. **Result detail level.** `TickContext.detail` is `"full"` (default) or `"summary"`.
   `summary` is for callers that never read the per-day result diagnostics:
   - market-day result lists stay in production order instead of being sorted, and the
     settlement's per-transaction copy is omitted;
   - the per-good needs detail is omitted;
   - the world outcome, postings, balances and totals are identical.
   The worker host uses `summary`; tests and benchmarks use `full` unless they explicitly
   measure the host path. A 33-day trace is identical at both levels, and the 92-day bench
   reports identical canonical hashes from the host (`summary`) and phase (`full`) runs.
5. **RNG purpose memoisation.** The keyed RNG caches the validated purpose prefix (the
   purpose validation and its two FNV lanes) in a bounded map of 1,024 entries. It is a
   derived value only: every draw returns exactly the same value with or without the cache,
   no authoritative state is stored, and arbitrary purpose strings cannot grow it without
   limit. Known-answer RNG tests are unchanged.
6. **Snapshot presentation cache.** The host owns a `SnapshotCache` that is never part of
   the world, a save or a hash. It holds the static snapshot sections per definitions
   object, per-summary government totals (closed summaries are immutable; compaction
   replaces the object), and the player's finance history until a summary, the account
   registry or the country's blocks change. Price indexes are derived once per snapshot.
   A cached snapshot is JSON-identical to an uncached one (test), and country summaries
   attribute blocks and assignments through the province-owner map instead of a per-block
   scan.
7. **Save format 2 and the stored form.** `SAVE_FORMAT_VERSION` is 2. Accounts, pop
   blocks, buildings, employment, provinces and period account totals are written as
   column tables; the payload is canonicalised once and the checksum is computed over that
   text (and verified over the text when the layout is ours, so a save does not need a
   second canonicalisation on load). Format-1 saves are listed as incompatible with the
   reason shown to the player; they are never deleted or overwritten (D-120). The stored
   form is `fon-save-deflate2:` + the envelope metadata as one JSON line + the canonical
   save deflated at level 1 (fflate, pure JavaScript on web/iOS/Android) + base64. The
   worker produces the stored form in `SAVE_RESULT` (compression runs off the app thread)
   and accepts it on `LOAD`/`PREVIEW_SAVE`; `compressSaveForStorage` is idempotent. Legacy
   `century-save-gzip1:` saves are still decoded for listing and incompatibility
   explanation, but are not loadable worlds.
8. **Worker protocol 18.** The version reflects the stored save form on `SAVE_RESULT` and
   `LOAD` (17 was the P2 province snapshot vocabulary). A client and worker must agree on
   the protocol version; older commands are rejected with `unsupported_protocol`.
9. **Dropped SUSD normalizer.** Save format 2 rejects every format-1 save (including
   pre-M14 saves whose persisted finance keys still said `SUSD`) before decoding, so the
   format-1 legacy-key normalizer was removed. The historical SUSD spelling survives only
   in the state-hash view and the opening-balance checksum, where it is required to keep
   historical hashes stable (M14 keeps the former spelling in hashes by design).
10. **m14 monetary-terminology audit rule.** The audit now requires the format-1
    incompatibility message in `serialization.ts` instead of the removed normalizer, and
    still requires the canonical `balanceRUSD` save field. The RUSD rename rule itself is
    unchanged: current player-facing and current documentation text must not say SUSD;
    historical records and legacy aliases may.

### Additional bit-identical savings recorded under this decision

- `postJournalBatch` validates and applies every entry before materialising any posting,
  and copies lines only for the tail that survives the 256-entry audit buffer (a
  settlement day creates several thousand wage/upkeep entries). The batch path adds line
  deltas directly into the batch aggregate instead of a per-entry map. A rejected batch
  leaves balances, postings and `nextPostingId` unchanged.
- Settlement reads opening producer/household cash from one dense account-id table (map
  fallback for sparse ids) and relies on the aggregated posting batch, which applies the
  same net market deltas, for the affordability invariant instead of a second
  hundred-thousand-account pass.
- `inIdOrder` uses the fact that `popBlocks`/`buildings`/`countries` are kept in ascending
  id order (construction appends only higher ids): a phase that wants an explicit order
  checks it in O(n) and only copies and sorts when a caller supplied an unsorted fixture.
- Household and production demand lists are emitted in canonical order by construction,
  so the two per-day 30k-record sorts and the per-building recipe copy/sort are gone.

### Consequences

- World outcomes, hashes and the 64-day reference trace are unchanged by every item above
  (checked with `docs/perf/check-trace.sh`); save text changes because format 2 is a new
  layout and a format break (D-120).
- The acceptance benchmark improves from host day p50 2,190 to about 550 ms, save and load
  to about 1.0-1.3 s, and new game to about 0.64 s. The remaining distance to 10x is
  measured and explained in `docs/archive/completion-reports/PERF_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`: most of it is fixed per-day work
  in settlement, market clearing, demography and needs, plus JavaScript deflate time on a
  15M-character canonical save, not a duplicate-validation or allocation bug that one more
  pass would remove.
- The worker bundle is 4,833 KiB (the generated 1900 scenario module dominates); this is
  recorded, not changed, in this stage.

## D-138 — Austria-Hungary white and Venezuela vinotinto on the political map

**Status:** implemented on 2026-11-03 at the designer's request.

### Context

The designer asked for Austria-Hungary to read as white and Venezuela to take its
vinotinto wine red on the map, pointing to the political colours of Victoria 3. The
palette had been deliberately matte and dark so paper-coloured labels and province lines
would read on every fill (D-112), and its contrast test assumed paper labels throughout.

### Decision

1. **Austria-Hungary is the one light fill.** `AUT` uses `#d3dcf0`, the Victoria 3 default
   Austrian colour: a cool near-white rather than pure white, so it still reads as a
   country rather than a hole in the paper. It remains authored per tag like every other
   colour, so no other polity's colour changes.
2. **Venezuela is vinotinto.** `VEN` uses `#7c2529`, a dark wine red in the matte band.
   It passes the unchanged neighbour-separation test under normal and simulated
   deuteranopic vision.
3. **Labels choose their ink from the fill.** `mapLabelInk` in `design/tokens.ts` keeps the
   paper label while it reaches the 2:1 floor and takes the dark chrome ink otherwise.
   The WebGL painter asks the engine for the fill under each label's text, sampled from the
   active style table so map modes and dimming are respected. A capital's name sits beside
   its dot, so it is sampled at the centre of the name, not the dot: Vaduz's dot is in
   Liechtenstein but its name lies on Austria-Hungary, and it takes the dark ink there.
   The SVG fallback makes the same choice in political mode. Sea labels stay paper. This keeps the original legibility
   floor rather than exempting Austria-Hungary from it.
4. **The atlas was regenerated** with `pnpm map:atlas`, because the runtime and the
   `tokensSha256` manifest changed.

### Assumptions and provisional values

The shades are display-only and provisional. The near-white is a deliberate exception to
the matte band, not a new rule for other countries; a second light fill should be checked
against the same label-ink test before it is added.

### Consequences

- The political map now matches the requested reference: a pale Habsburg white in central
  Europe and a vinotinto Venezuela in northern South America.
- Province and national lines remain paper. They are faint on the white but visible, and
  the dark country and province labels carry the legibility. D-112's label floor is now
  tested against the ink actually used, for every fill and era, and
  `m16-map-label-ink.test.ts` drives the painter to check the ink each glyph is drawn in.
- A name that straddles a border still takes one ink, chosen from the fill at its centre;
  its ends may sit on the other fill. This is accepted for short capital names.
- The 844 × 390 atlas was checked in desktop Chrome on an NVIDIA GTX 750 Ti; the two
  requested countries render as intended (`docs/images/living-atlas/palette-austria-hungary.png`
  and `palette-venezuela.png`). Those captures predate the text-centre sampling, so Vaduz
  shows in faint paper there; after the fix it was rechecked at 844 × 390 in the desktop
  app's built-in browser, where Vaduz and the Bosnian province names draw in the dark ink.
  Device behaviour remains the designer's test.

## D-139 — P2b and the first performance stage accepted; 10× is mandatory before P3

**Status:** decided by the designer on 2026-11-04.

### Decision

1. **P2b is accepted as delivered** (D-135, D-136), including the price rule as
   implemented (opening stock plus same-day output less producers' funded input demand)
   rather than the earlier "opening stock only" wording, and its listed open items.
2. **The first performance stage (D-137) is accepted as an interim result.** Its 10×
   target is not waived: the stage continues, and **10× against the D-137 baseline is a
   precondition for P3 and every later Prosperity stage.** The baseline is
   `docs/perf/baseline.json` (host day mean 2,356 ms, p50 2,190 ms; save 5,364 ms; load
   5,107 ms; new game 990 ms, 5× target).
3. **Architecture may be rethought** to reach it, and **save compatibility may be broken**
   without migration: the game is in development. Determinism, conservation, validation
   of authoritative state and the No-Mana doctrine are not relaxed.
4. **Working copies are LF.** `.gitattributes` pins `eol=lf`. The two `m11-flags` checks
   failed only because `core.autocrlf` checked LF files out as CRLF while the generator
   emits LF; no generated content was stale.

### Second performance stage (2026-11-04): what changed

Every change below keeps the world outcome **bit-identical** to the D-137 baseline: the
64-day reference trace (`docs/perf/trace-baseline.txt`) is reproduced line for line in
full and in summary result detail. Bit identity was kept deliberately as the oracle for a
large internal rewrite; save compatibility was not needed and was not broken.

1. **Columnar market core.** Market clearing, production and storage overflow work on a
   per-market lot table (`LotTable`: a dense `(good, owner)` table replacing string-keyed
   and nested lot maps) and typed demand and reservation columns. `runProductionPhase`
   and the market day share one production core (`runProductionCore`), so there is still
   one implementation. Allocation, eligibility and every sum keep their operands and
   order.
2. **Columns between phases.** Household demand is built as columns and passed to the
   market as `MarketDayInput.householdColumns` (validated with the record checks and
   messages). The market returns transaction and fulfilment columns as a non-enumerable
   companion of its result (`MarketDayResult.columns`); settlement and the needs phase
   read them, and a result whose `pendingTransactions` list was replaced (as tests do to
   build malformed results) is settled from its objects. Record objects are built only
   for `detail: "full"`.
3. **Settlement.** Transactions are ordered by a stable LSD radix sort over decimal-text
   ranks, which reproduces the canonical `transactionKey` order without string keys; market
   postings are aggregated with typed scratch in the same summation order and applied
   from line columns (`postJournalColumns`, the checks and per-account summation order of
   `postJournalBatch`). Their audit copies are materialised only if they can still be among
   the newest 256 postings once the day's wage and upkeep postings are known
   (`retainDeferredPostings`); the buffer ends exactly as eager posting would leave it.
   The integrated day passes `atomic: false`, because the rollback runner (phase mode) or
   the staged copy (staged mode) already owns atomicity; direct callers keep the
   100k-account rollback copy.
4. **Finance index.** Owner/subtype lookups resolve through owner tables built in one pass
   (`FinanceIndex.ownerAccountIds`, `position`); journal batches accumulate deltas in a
   typed array over account positions in first-touch order instead of a map.
5. **Allocation discipline.** Hot `for...of` loops became indexed loops (iterator results
   were about a fifth of the bytes allocated per day); the 16 rest-parameter tolerance
   helpers became fixed-arity; semantic-rule checks use a precomputed table; phases the
   integrated day calls skip re-validating the economy definitions it validated at its
   start (the existing staged-mode pattern of the production phase).
6. **Demography.** Effective mortality rates are formed per age band and fertility rates
   per day, with the same expressions.
7. **First new game.** The worker host builds `createEconomyWorld(baseScenario, seed)` at
   startup for the menus. While that world is untouched, the first `CREATE_GAME` sets
   `playerCountryId` on it instead of rebuilding and revalidating the 1900 world (the
   construction differs only in that field); any tick, action or load ends the reuse, and
   later games are built afresh. `d139-new-game-reuse.test.ts` pins the equality. The
   construction cost moves to worker startup, behind the title screen.

New probes in `packages/bench`: `perfPhases` (per-phase day), `perfGc` (GC per day),
`perfAlloc` (sampled allocation), `perfHost` (STEP profile), `perfSave` (save/load stages).

### Second performance stage: status (2026-11-04)

**10× is not yet reached.** Measured on the 92-day acceptance run: host day mean 356 ms
(6.6×) and p50 303 ms (7.2×); save 1,094 ms (4.9×); load 1,173 ms (4.4×); first new
campaign 63 ms (15.7×, the world is built at worker start); month starts 3.08–3.53× the
ordinary p50 (the ≤ 3× criterion, met after stage 1, is not met now because ordinary days
improved more). Determinism, bit identity, staged = phase and the save round trip pass;
1,337 tests pass. Details, the measured remaining costs and the proposed next steps are in
`docs/archive/completion-reports/PERF_STAGE2_REPORT.md`. Per item 2 above, P3 stays blocked until 10× is reached or the
designer accepts an exception.

### Designer decision (2026-11-04): performance stage accepted as complete

The designer accepted the second performance stage as complete for now and **lifted the
10× precondition of item 2 above**: P3 and the later Prosperity stages may start on the
measured result (host day 6.6× mean / 7.2× p50, save 4.9×, load 4.4×, first campaign
15.7×, month starts 3.08–3.53× the ordinary day; see `docs/archive/completion-reports/PERF_STAGE2_REPORT.md`). The
unmet targets and the proposed next steps stay recorded there as open performance work;
new stages should not make the measured costs worse without saying so in their reports.

## D-140 — Proposed: money as metal and paper, currency value changes, currency switching

**Status:** recorded at the designer's instruction on 2026-11-04 during P3; **proposed, not
assigned**. The mechanics below belong to later stages and are not implemented by P3.
**Amends (when accepted):** `docs/PROSPERITY_PROGRAM.md` P5 (deposits), P6 (goods
catalogue) and P11 (banks and credit); GDD §17J.3.

### Designer's requirements (2026-11-04)

1. Gold and silver are goods: they are mined, traded and can change price.
2. The value of every currency fluctuates (inflation and deflation); RUSD alone is
   constant by design (D-122).
3. Each polity may give up its independent currency, and an independent country without
   one may create one.

### Proposed model

- **Metal goods (P5/P6).** `gold` and `silver` join the goods catalogue with mass units
  (fine kilograms); gold and silver deposits join P5's deposit list (USGS MRDS plus authored
  historical fields, D-121), with depletion conserved. Industrial use and hoarding are
  demand; mints and monetary authorities are buyers and sellers.
- **Metal standards.** A gold- or silver-standard currency is defined by its legal fine
  content per unit. Its RUSD value is fine content × the metal's market price, held near the
  mint parity by coinage and melting (the gold and silver points as explicit arbitrage
  flows, not a clamp). Monetary reserves are held as metal stock as well as RUSD.
- **Paper.** An inconvertible currency's value follows issuance against reserves and money
  demand (with P11's banks and credit); a convertible paper currency is held at its metal
  parity while reserves last, and is suspended when they do not.
- **Switching.** Player and AI actions: an `own` country may adopt another currency
  (redeeming its circulation at the current valuation into the adopted currency, with the
  conversion posted through the existing accounts); an independent country with an
  `adopted` arrangement may establish its own currency (a new `Currency` record, an
  authority and an opening issue against reserves). Colonies and dependencies follow their
  overlord's arrangement.
- **No-Mana.** Every valuation change is caused by metal prices, issuance, reserves or
  policy; nothing accumulates unconditionally.

### What P3 already provides for it (D-141)

- Each 1900 currency is recorded with its standard, metal and fine content per unit
  (`historical/p3/currencies_1900.json`); its opening `rusdPerUnit` is derived from them.
- Countries that used another state's coin in 1900 start with an `adopted` arrangement;
  the Maria Theresa thaler is a foreign-issued currency.
- The display reads valuations and arrangements from every snapshot, so it follows value
  changes and arrangement changes without assuming a fixed rate; history figures convert
  at the valuation of their own month.

## D-141 — P3: 1900 currency parities, arrangements and local-currency display

**Status:** implemented for review on 2026-11-04 under the designer's resolutions given in
the P3 session (metal and paper money as data, adopted arrangements, paper at the 1900
circulating rate, history at its own rate; the money mechanics themselves are D-140).
**Implements:** D-122 §3 and §4, GDD §17J.2 (6), `docs/PROSPERITY_PROGRAM.md` §4 P3.

### Decision

1. **Data, not logic.** `packages/data/data/historical/p3/currencies_1900.json` records,
   for every country of the 1900 world, its arrangement (own or adopted) and for each own
   currency its standard (`gold`, `silver`, `paper`), the metal of its legal unit, how its
   value is derived and its sources. `packages/data/src/currencies.ts` validates the file
   and derives the fine content, the 1900 circulating value in US gold dollars and
   `rusdPerUnit = value × CPI(1990)/CPI(1900)` (15.704, the D-130 deflator). The scenario
   file names it as `meta.files.currencies`; it supersedes the currency blocks of the
   generated `historical/p2b/countries_1900.json` for the 1900 world only. The M5 sample
   keeps its unavailable valuations (D-080).
2. **Sources.** Director of the Mint, *Values of Foreign Coins, January 1, 1901* (pp. 56–57,
   read from the page scans) for the legal value of each unit; the same report's foreign
   answers on actual currency, premium on gold and exchange on London in 1900 for paper
   currencies; its monetary-systems chapter for the Latin Union pars and the Siamese
   tical (5 ticals = 3 Mexican dollars); the Gold Standard Act of 1900 for the gold price
   (1.504632 g fine per dollar). Silver is priced at the Mint's own October–December 1900
   valuation (Mexican dollar 0.509 / 24.4388 g fine = 0.6478 USD per troy ounce).
3. **Paper at its 1900 circulating rate.** Where the Mint report gives a 1900 premium on
   gold or a London quotation the currency opens at `legal value / (1 + premium)` (or the
   pence quotation): Argentina 130.95 %, Spain 32.53 % (mean monthly exchange premium),
   Portugal 37.96 d per 1,000 reis, Greece 58 %, Serbia 11 % (midpoint 8–14 %), Liberia 25 %,
   Austria-Hungary 0.775 %, Paraguay 694 % (midpoint); Guatemala 510 %, Salvador 137 %,
   Nicaragua 180 % and Haiti 112 % against the US gold dollar. This replaces the proposed
   "Mint parity everywhere" (R1a): currencies fluctuate from the start (D-140).
4. **Adopted arrangements.** Countries that used another state's coin in 1900 adopt it:
   Montenegro and Liechtenstein the Austro-Hungarian crown; Andorra and Monaco the franc;
   San Marino the lira; the Australian colonies, New Zealand, Tonga and the Orange Free
   State sterling; Ethiopia, Oman, Wadai, Darfur, Ha'il and Sokoto the Maria Theresa thaler,
   a foreign-issued currency (`issuer.kind = "foreign"`, id `FOREIGN_CURRENCY_ID_BASE` =
   1000). 52 own currencies and the thaler remain (53). Own currencies keep the country id.
   Adopted countries have no monetary authority (E6b), so 90 monetary accounts fewer are
   allocated and later account ids shift; balances are identical (item 8).
5. **Names.** Anachronistic names are corrected (keys unchanged): Honduran, Nicaraguan,
   Salvadoran and Paraguayan peso, Nepalese mohar, Kabuli rupee, Siamese tical, Persian
   kran, Haikwan tael; placeholder currencies of the adopting states are removed.
6. **Month-end valuations (schema 30).** Every finance-period close records the specified
   valuations (`FinancePeriodSummary.closingValuations`, ascending currency id, validated,
   serialized, kept through compaction). Reporting-only: no simulation decision reads them.
   The snapshot carries them for the ledger window and the mean of each reported year
   (`SimulationSnapshot.valuationHistory`, protocol 19), with `CountrySnapshot.currencyId`
   and `monetaryArrangement`.
7. **One formatter.** `packages/app/src/format/money.ts` converts RUSD to the display
   currency at display time (`display = rusd / rusdPerUnit`); history converts at its
   month's closing valuation or its year's mean, falling back to the current valuation
   when none was recorded. Every money label in the app uses it (`useMoney()`); the
   sim-worker display helpers (explanations, action descriptions, debt and construction
   previews, route description) take a required `MoneyText` and print no unit of their own.
   Money sliders show and edit display units with a 1-2-5 step and report RUSD; typed
   amounts are converted to RUSD at the command boundary, so commands stay RUSD. Rates
   (valuations, parities) stay in RUSD per unit. Dispatch headlines keep the amount and
   format it when shown.
8. **No feedback.** The display preference ("own", "rusd" or `currency:<key>`) is a
   per-device AsyncStorage value, never in a save or the world; the worker protocol has no
   display command. `node packages/bench/dist/p3Equivalence.js 35` shows, for 35 days of the
   1900 world including the first month close: with the same arrangements, the world with
   parities equals the world without valuations bit for bit once the valuation fields are
   copied across; against the pre-P3 world, every simulated quantity (balances by owner and
   subtype, population, buildings, markets, prices, trade, construction, politics,
   technology, military, period totals) is identical.
9. **Settings.** A Settings panel in the campaign sheet and a Settings entry on the title
   screen hold the display-currency selector: the player's own currency (default), RUSD,
   and every valued registered currency; rows are ≥ 44 pt, token-styled, without hover.

### Provisional values and missing sources

- **Paper discount not sourced (metal parity stands):** Italy, Chile, Brazil (answer: rate
  not at hand), Colombia (paper money, discount not given), Bulgaria, Ecuador (ambiguous
  answer "108, 105, 107 average").
- **No 1900 value of the own unit:** Afghanistan and Nepal valued at the Indian rupee;
  the Dominican Republic at the Central American silver peso; Korea at par with the yen
  (Mint report: Japanese money circulates, exchange with Japan at ½ %); Luxembourg at the
  franc; Paraguay's gold reference at the Argentine gold peso.
- **Adoption choices:** Andorra (franc chosen over the peseta), Oman (thaler over the
  Indian rupee), Sokoto, Wadai, Darfur and Ha'il (thaler; cowries are not representable).
  The thaler's fine content (23.389 g) is the convention standard (secondary source).
- **OCR-read answers:** Spain, Guatemala, Nicaragua, Salvador, Haiti, Liberia, Serbia,
  Paraguay and Austria-Hungary were read from the archive.org OCR text; Argentina, Greece,
  Portugal and the coin tables from the page scans.
- **Colonial currencies** (Indian rupee, Straits dollar, Dutch Indies guilder, colonial
  francs) are not registered: a colony is part of its overlord's country and shows the
  overlord's currency. Per-province currencies are out of scope.

### Consequences

- The 1900 world hash changes once (valuations, arrangements, names, closing valuations);
  schema 29 saves are incompatible (D-120, D-139 §3).
- Silver currencies keep their opening RUSD value until D-140's metal prices exist; a
  player can now establish a monetary authority at the sourced parity (E6b).

## D-142 — P4: cause-driven demography, sourced 1900 levels and urban share

**Status:** implemented for review on 2026-11-05 under the designer's go-ahead to the P4
pre-build report (same day), with the added requirement that the province and nation
ledgers show population pyramids and that mortality, fertility and population can change
and be influenced by the player.
**Implements:** D-124, GDD §17J.2 (5), `docs/PROSPERITY_PROGRAM.md` §4 P4.
**Supersedes:** the year-indexed world schedules as the level of fertility and mortality
(D-026/D-027 inputs, GDD §6.6) and the M2 unmet-needs mortality terms.
**Amended by:** D-144 (§2: the fertility level is the natural-fertility saturation of the
causal product).

### Decision

1. **Pattern and level.** `demography.json` holds one *standard schedule*: the WPP 2024
   world 1950 ASFR (TFR 4.83) and sex-specific central death rates by five-year band. It is
   only the age pattern. Each block has a fertility level (multiplier of the standard
   ASFR) and a child (0–4) and an adult (5+) mortality level (multipliers of the standard
   death rates). Two mortality levels let a province reproduce both its sourced life
   expectancy and its under-5 mortality, and give fertility its child-mortality input.
   The levels are **derived every day** from the block's fixed residuals and its current
   causes; they are not stored, so a save holds no cached copy.
2. **Causes** (all coefficients in `population/constants.ts` with units, justification and
   PROVISIONAL status):
   - fertility level = residual × exp(−0.6 × literacy of women 15–49) × (1 − 0.25 × urban
     share) × (income ÷ subsistence)^−0.1 × (lagged child mortality)^0.35 × (1 −
     contraception) × (1 − 0.8 × (1 − food sufficiency));
   - child / adult mortality = residual × (1 + 0.5 / 0.3 × urban share × (1 − sanitation
     coverage)) × H^1.5 / H × (1 + 3 / 1.5 × (1 − food sufficiency)), H being the existing
     M9/E5 combined health factor (medical technology × public-health service × statutory
     hours);
   - **famine lowers births** (0.8 per unit of calorie shortfall: −40 % at half the
     requirement; Ó Gráda 2009). Not in D-124's list; added after the first century run,
     in which the lagged child-mortality term alone drove TFR to 8.3 and the crude birth
     rate to 67 per 1,000 during the persistent 1900s food collapse;
   - lagged child mortality: exponential smoothing of the effective child multiplier,
     mean lag 60 months, updated at each month start; food sufficiency: calories actually
     bought today ÷ 1,950 kcal per person, capped at 1 and smoothed over 30 days; both are
     stored at `RATE_DECIMAL_PLACES` (9) like other stored rates;
   - sanitation coverage = sanitation knowledge (new technology effect `urban-sanitation`:
     basic-sanitation 0.3, public-health-administration 0.1, water-treatment 0.4,
     environmental-health 0.2) × E5 public-health service output; contraception = new
     effect `contraception` (family-planning-programs 0.15).
   No level accumulates by itself: with constant causes the levels are constant (tested).
3. **Residuals.** At world creation each block's residual is fixed so that, under its
   country's start-of-game factors and full food, its levels equal the sourced 1900 levels.
   The residual stands for persistent factors the model does not simulate (marriage
   pattern, breastfeeding, disease environment). It never changes; migrants join the
   destination block's residual.
4. **Measured from existing state** (deviations): literacy is not sex-specific, so the
   literacy of the 15–49 cohorts stands in for female literacy; income relative to
   subsistence is recovered from the smoothed wealth indicator (GDD §6 allows wealth to act
   on birth and death rates when documented); there is no family-law slot and no direct law
   factor (education law acts through literacy, health law through E5 services); housing
   and crowding wait for P6, so the urban penalty is the crowding effect; war acts on
   civilians only through food (soldier deaths stay in the military model); there is no
   famine switch — famine is food shortfall.
5. **Sources.** Per province, the modern country under its own point (Natural Earth
   admin-0 crosswalk, `historical/p4/province_modern_country_1900.json`; 21 coastal points
   by nearest boundary) or an authored stand-in (`proxies_1900.json`, 55 entries, low
   confidence); Gapminder life expectancy v15, babies per woman v15, under-5 mortality v12
   and Systema Globalis crude birth rate at pinned commits (`gapminder_1900.json`). The
   **1900 level is the 1896–1905 mean** (at least five years): single years carry crises
   (India's 1899–1900 famine: life expectancy 19.5 against about 24–25 around it; Finland
   in 1900). Microstate Gapminder TFRs for 1900 (Andorra 2.11, Monaco 3.06, San Marino
   2.82, Liechtenstein 2.71) are modern values carried back and are rejected. 182 source
   geos; 74 provinces by stand-in.
6. **Age structure.** Each province starts from the stable population of its sourced
   fertility and mortality (Lotka r), replacing the single world pyramid; totals unchanged.
7. **Urban/rural.** Each block carries an urban share: a province's HYDE 3.3 urban people go
   first to its clerk, laborer, academic and capitalist blocks, then to peasants. Fixed until
   P7 moves people between town and country.
8. **Acceptance tolerances** (documented in the generated file): crude birth rate ±15 % of
   Gapminder per 1900 country; life expectancy ±1 year, under-5 mortality and TFR ±5 % per
   source geo. **Exceptions:** Greece and Crete — Gapminder's Greek crude birth rate (30.5)
   is inconsistent with its own TFR 5.2 and life expectancy 40.9 (about 38 implied), the
   mark of under-registered births. **Crude death rate:** no open per-country 1900 source
   exists (Gapminder and OWID start in 1950, the Human Mortality Database requires a login,
   Mitchell is not accessible); the target is the rate implied by the sourced life
   expectancy and under-5 mortality in the stable population. A designer-supplied Mitchell
   table would replace it.
9. **WPP is validation only.** The WPP 1950–2000 annual world schedules moved to
   `validation/wpp_world_schedules_1950_2000.json` and WPP 2024 regional 1975/2000 figures to
   `validation/wpp_regions_1975_2000.json`; a test asserts no simulation source reads them.
   The M2 engine test feeds the annual schedules to the births/deaths/ageing engine itself
   (levels held at 1) and still requires WPP's 1975 and 2000 structure.
10. **Migration fix (regression).** Migration removed migrants from the source block
    without re-deriving its 15+ literacy aggregate, so from the first migration month
    (day 31) full validation — and with it every save — failed, on `main` as well
    (aggregate off by about 1e-7 against a 1e-8 tolerance). The source is now re-synced,
    and a failed migration also restores per-age literacy. Test:
    `p4-demography` "validates and saves after its first migration month".
11. **Reporting.** `World.demography` records per country and civil year realised births and
    deaths, start and end population, and TFR, life expectancy, under-5 mortality and urban
    share at the turn of the year (reporting only; schema 31). The snapshot (protocol 20)
    carries, for the player and focused countries, rates, pyramid, causes and annual
    history, and for one focused province (new presentation-only command
    `SET_PROVINCE_FOCUS`) its rates and pyramid — per-province columns for all 1,687
    provinces would have pushed the snapshot past the 700k-character budget. The province
    panel and the Population ledger show them with a pyramid and a yearly chart, and name
    the player's levers.

### Provisional values and open items

- Every P4 coefficient is provisional (P15 calibrates against WPP 1950–2000).
- Food energy per kg (grain 3,400, livestock 2,300, fish 1,000, food-products 2,500 kcal)
  and the 1,950 kcal requirement are provisional.
- The M2 population-only aggregate starts from the population-weighted 1900 world levels
  (TFR 5.52, life expectancy 34.6, under-5 mortality 399); with no causes to move them it
  overshoots the 1950 total, which the restated M2.1 test documents instead of keeping the
  former 1.45 mortality scaling (D-124 forbids forcing a trajectory).
- The eight-polity M5 sample likewise starts from the 1900 world levels
  (`meta.worldDemography`). Its fish and livestock capacity sits at demand in two markets,
  so the binary unmet-days share is 25 % on day 31 although 99.86 % of what is paid for is
  supplied; `m5-feasibility` now checks the two causes of a structural shortage (supply
  under 99.5 % of paid demand; population below 95 % food sufficiency under 10 %) and
  bounds the binary share at 30 %. **Restated for designer review.**
- Data version stays `m3-economy-v12` (as in P2–P3); saves break through schema 31.

### Consequences

- 1900 starts differ by province and country; the world opens at crude birth rate 41.2,
  crude death rate 30.4, TFR 5.52, life expectancy 32.9 and 16.6 % urban.
- The pre-existing household-income spiral of the 1900 world (grain piles up while
  households afford under half their basket by month ten; present on `main`, where the
  unmet-days terms drive deaths to about 91 million in the first year) now acts through
  calories. P4 does not hide it; its cause belongs to P5–P7 (own consumption, wages). See
  `P4_COMPLETION_REPORT.md`.
- Every world hash changes; schema-30 saves are incompatible (D-120, D-139 §3).

## D-143 — P5: land, harvests, own consumption, resources and the full goods catalogue

**Status:** in progress (2026-11-05). The designer approved the P5 pre-build report
("looks good"), then asked for the whole 1900–2000 goods catalogue in P5, adding tin, gold
and silver and splitting consumer electronics and refined fuels (57 goods; proposal
`docs/P5_GOODS_CATALOGUE_PROPOSAL.md`).
**Implements:** `docs/PROSPERITY_PROGRAM.md` §4 P5, GDD §17J.3 P5, D-121; brings the goods
catalogue of P6 forward (P6 keeps the linear-expenditure demand, saving and housing).

### Approved basis (pre-build report)

1. **Farm output** = P2b output × staffing^0.6 × (land share ÷ 1900 land share)^0.4 ×
   harvest weather × technology. A province's cropland (pasture for ranches) is shared
   among its farms in proportion to their levels. At world creation each farm's technique
   is divided by its 1900 land factor, so 1900 output is unchanged and nothing new is
   stored. Factor shares labour 0.6 / land 0.4 (Clark 2002), provisional.
2. **Own consumption.** Each peasant block keeps the subsistence quantities of the food its
   own farms make before anything is sold, spread over those farms by their day's output.
   No cash moves: the kept food, valued at the opening price, is first a wage in kind
   (the farm's cash wage to that block falls by it), then an owner's draw. The kept
   calories count toward P4 food sufficiency.
3. **Harvest variance:** keyed draw `harvest.weather` per (province, calendar year),
   lognormal with mean 1 and σ 0.15 (provisional), for farms and ranches.
4. **Deposits:** sparse per (province, resource) state with size, remaining stock, quality
   and a discovered flag; hidden deposits need a prospecting technology and are then
   found by a keyed monthly draw `resource.discovery` scaled by province infrastructure;
   extraction depletes them through one validated function.
5. **Day-6,320 failure:** storage overflow shrank unsold lots geometrically into subnormal
   quantities (first subnormal lots in 1911 on `main`); a sale of a 1.5e-323 kg lot has
   price × quantity = 0. Lots that would fall below the quantity tolerance are removed
   whole and recorded as overflow loss.
6. **Saves:** only the latest monthly summary keeps the per-account trial balance;
   compacted months keep per-country closing treasury, debt and household cash (what the
   Ledger history reads).

### Implemented in P5a (2026-11-05)

1. **State (schema 32).** `World.resources` holds the deposits (`economy/resourceState.ts`:
   one per province and resource; `initialStock`, cumulative `extracted`, `quality`,
   `discovered`, `discoveredOnDay`, `requiredTechnologyId`). Remaining stock is
   `initialStock − extracted`, so depletion is conserved by construction; validation
   rejects negative, excessive or hidden-but-extracted states. Saves store the deposits as
   columns. `BuildingDef.landUse` (cropland, pasture) is content, set only in the 1900
   definitions; the legacy definitions (M5 sample, fixtures) farm no land and keep their
   behaviour apart from own consumption.
2. **Farm output** (`economy/agriculture.ts`, `production.ts`): staffing^0.6 × land
   factor × harvest weather replaces the linear staffing ratio of land-using buildings; the
   land factor is `(hectares per level ÷ 1,000)^0.4`, land shared by operational levels per
   province and kind; world creation divides each farm's technique by its starting land
   factor. A farm in a province without land of its kind produces nothing; world creation
   and construction reject such placements.
3. **Own consumption** (`planOwnConsumption`): computed before demand from the day's
   capacity results; production diverts the planned quantity from the owner's lot
   (`BuildingProductionResult.outputs[].ownConsumption`); household demand is reduced by
   it; the kept calories count in food sufficiency; settlement pays its value at opening
   prices as a wage in kind to the owner block (`WagePayment.paidInKindRUSD`), the rest
   being the owner's draw. The day result lists the entries (`economy.ownConsumption`).
   It applies to any population block that privately owns an agriculture building without
   inputs that makes a subsistence food.
4. **Discovery** (`economy/resources.ts`): on the first civil day of a month, each hidden
   deposit whose province owner knows its technology is found with probability
   `RESOURCE_DISCOVERY_MONTHLY_HAZARD` (1/120) × province infrastructure, keyed draw
   `resource.discovery` per (deposit, month). `extractFromDeposit` is the only depletion
   path; extraction buildings arrive with the goods catalogue (P5b).
5. **Data** (`scripts/p5/`; provenance `docs/P5_LAND_AND_RESOURCES_PROVENANCE.md`): land and
   imputation, 3,834 deposits from the authored table, USGS MRDS and SAGE/HYDE forest,
   three prospecting technologies and 16 survey countries; the runtime reads the P5
   overlays of the P2b provinces, countries and building definitions.
6. **Saves:** `MAX_DETAILED_PERIOD_SUMMARIES` 12 → 1 with per-country closing balances kept
   through compaction (Ledger history unchanged).
7. **Snapshot (protocol 21):** province ownership as columns (`provinceOwnership`,
   `provinceOwnershipRows`), which removed 214k characters; a `harvestFactor` column (the
   Harvest lens, map bridge protocol 3); the focused province's `land` section (harvest,
   farm output and kept food today, known deposits; hidden deposits are never sent).
8. **Day-6,320 fix:** `applyOverflowToTable` removes a lot that would fall below the
   quantity tolerance and records it as loss (regression tests in `p5-land`).
9. **Trade funding fix (found by the P5 five-year run).** On day 460 trade dispatch let an
   owner commit 6,248.601058167046 RUSD against a balance of 6,248.601058167045: the day's
   trade postings are one journal batch that sums each account's lines, while the cash
   tracked per shipment is a sequence of differences. `shipmentFunding` now keeps a margin
   of 16 ulps (at least of 1 RUSD; below 1e-10 RUSD at 1900 scale) on the enterprise's and
   the owner's cash, and the owner's injection covers the cost plus that margin (the
   extra stays in the enterprise; conserved). Pre-existing; P5's different state exposed it.
10. **Measured saves (stored characters, 1900 world, same seed):** day 3 — main 2.87 M,
    P5 3.00 M (+4 %, the deposits); day 92 — 5.22 M → 4.28 M (−18 %); day 366 —
    11.03 M → 4.67 M (−58 %). The web save still fails: the save library writes a staging
    copy and the primary (and keeps the previous one), so even 2.9 M exceeds Chrome's
    ~5.24 M quota from the first save, on `main` as well. A storage-backend change is not
    started (designer's instruction).

### Restated tests (designer review)

- `m5-feasibility`: supply criterion 99.5 % → 99 % of what a block pays for. Owners keep
  their farms' subsistence food first, so in the two M5 markets whose fish and livestock
  capacity equals demand the buyers receive 99.28 % (P4: 99.86 %); food sufficiency stays 1.
- `batch5-validation`: `fallbackTicks > 0` became a reported value. Since D-125 a capped
  input demand adds no market attempt or validation; with wages partly paid in kind no
  input demand is capped in the measured window. The validation count (12) is unchanged.
- `e2-fiscal-management`, `m6-ai`, `m4b-save-compaction`: restated for the one-month
  detailed window (closed months keep exact fiscal totals, budgets, flows, valuations and
  closing balances; corruption of a compacted month is still caught at save boundaries).
- `p2b-money`, `batch6-integration`: an owner's wage counts cash plus in kind.
11. **Century run (P5 branch):** passed P4's 1917 stop and stopped on 1 February 1993 (day
    34,058) when the P4 validator rejected a block's derived fertility level of 4.009 (range
    0..4). Households without farms still starve, so towns and literacy collapse (2.4 % urban,
    7 % women literate by 1993), and P4's causal fertility rises with them. Left for the
    designer (a bound, a saturating response, or the economy fix first); not clamped.
    **Addressed by D-144** (natural-fertility saturation); not yet re-verified by a century run.

## D-144 — P4: natural-fertility ceiling of the fertility level

**Status:** implemented on 2026-11-05 on `prosperity/p5-land` at the designer's request;
tests to be revised by the designer; not yet re-verified by a century run (the designer
reruns the century after P10).
**Amends:** D-142 §2 (fertility level). **Follows:** D-143 item 11.

### Diagnosis

The no-input century run on the P5a branch stopped on 1 February 1993 (day 34,058) when
the P4 validator rejected a block's derived fertility level of 4.009
(`FERTILITY_LEVEL_MAX` = 4). D-142 derives the level as residual × F(causes), a product of
independent cause factors (literacy of women 15–49, urban share, income ÷ subsistence,
lagged child mortality, contraception, food), and nothing bounded the product. Households
without farms had starved for decades, so literacy (7 %), towns (2.4 %), income and child
mortality all moved that block's factors the same way. A level of 4.0 is about 19 children
per woman: above total fecundity (about 15.3, Bongaarts 1978) and far above any observed
population (Hutterites about 10–11, Eaton & Mayer 1953). Independent multiplicative
factors are reasonable near the observed range but compound without limit outside it,
whereas real fertility saturates at its biological ceiling. Raising the bound would allow
impossible fertility; clamping silently is forbidden (AGENTS.md).

### Decision

1. **Saturation.** The fertility level is derived as
   - raw = residual × F(causes) (unchanged, D-142 §2);
   - level = raw when raw ≤ KNEE;
   - level = KNEE + (CAP − KNEE) × tanh((raw − KNEE) / (CAP − KNEE)) when raw > KNEE.

   The function is continuous with slope 1 at the knee and strictly increasing, so a
   rising cause still raises fertility, ever less as it nears the ceiling. It is applied
   where the level is derived (`naturalFertilitySaturation` inside `fertilityLevel`), and
   the level is still never stored.
2. **Constants** (`population/constants.ts`, PROVISIONAL, P15 calibrates):
   - `FERTILITY_NATURAL_KNEE` = 1.7 (multiplier of the standard ASFR, TFR 4.83): about
     8.2 children per woman, the upper range of natural-fertility populations without
     parity-specific control (Henry 1961). Every sourced 1900 province level is ≤ 1.667,
     and the M2 and M5 world levels are 1.144.
   - `FERTILITY_NATURAL_CAP` = 2.3: about 11.1 children per woman, the top of the
     Hutterite range (about 10–11; Eaton & Mayer 1953), the highest recorded for a whole
     population, with headroom for its upper estimates, and well below total fecundity
     (about 15.3; Bongaarts 1978).
   - **Cap revised by the designer, 2026-11-05:** the first draft of this decision set the
     cap at 2.2 (about 10.6 children per woman, the central Hutterite figure). The
     designer raised it to 2.3 before anything was committed, so the ceiling sits at the
     top of the observed range rather than at its centre. The knee, the formula and the
     validation bound are unchanged; the saturation band (CAP − KNEE) widens from 0.5 to
     0.6, so above the knee the level rises slightly faster than in the draft (raw 2.5
     gives 2.222 instead of 2.161).
   - `FERTILITY_LEVEL_MAX` = 4 stays as the impossible-state validation bound; it is now
     unreachable through causes.
3. **Residual fitting.** `calibrateBlockDemography` fits the residual to the inverse of the
   saturation (`inverseNaturalFertilitySaturation`, an atanh above the knee), so a block
   reproduces its sourced level exactly through the saturated derivation. For every
   sourced 1900 level the inverse is the identity, so the 1900 residuals and levels are
   unchanged. A sourced level at or above the cap cannot arise from causes and is now
   rejected as an impossible state (none exists in the data or fixtures).
   `calibrateDemographyForCountries` reads the neutral-factor level through the same
   saturation and refits through the inverse, so the round trip is exact.
4. **Consistency.** Births (`population/demography.ts`), the indicators (TFR, crude birth
   rate; `population/indicators.ts`), the yearly demography report, the snapshot's current
   figures and the Population ledger all read `fertilityLevel`, so they all see the
   saturated level. The snapshot's demography causes are the country inputs (health
   factor, sanitation, contraception) and are unaffected. The profession fertility
   multipliers apply after the saturation; all are 1 in the data.

### Consequences

- Whatever the causes, a block's fertility can no longer exceed about 11.1 children per
  woman (2.3 × 4.83); the 1993 block's raw 4.009 now gives a level of 2.2995 (about 11.1).
- The 1900 start, every sourced level and every world hash up to the first day a block's
  raw level passes 1.7 are unchanged; no state, schema (32) or protocol (21) change.
- The cause behind the 1993 stop, the collapse of towns and literacy in households without
  farms, remains an economic problem for P5b–P10; the ceiling only keeps its demographic
  response possible.

## D-145 — Repository cleanup: tests by subsystem, historical documents archived

**Status:** implemented on 2026-11-06 on `prosperity/p5-land` at the designer's request,
from the approved proposal `docs/CLEANUP_PROPOSAL.md` (with the designer's choices
recorded there). No simulation, app or worker behaviour changes; no state, schema (32),
protocol (21) or data-version change.

### Decision

1. **Tests are organised by subsystem.** `packages/tests/src` has twelve folders —
   `architecture`, `determinism`, `serialization`, `population`, `economy`, `money`,
   `trade`, `government`, `data`, `map`, `ui-shell`, `worker` — instead of one flat folder
   named by milestone. Files were moved with `git mv`; only relative imports and paths
   built from `import.meta.url` changed in that step.
2. **Duplicated assertions are kept once.** Merges keep every assertion that still
   applies; nothing was weakened, skipped or rewritten to pass.
   - `serialization/save-compatibility.test.ts` holds the earlier-save rejections that
     were standalone tests in five files (schemas 2, 3, 6, 8, 9–12 with their simulation
     and content versions, same worlds and messages), and the stored schema-27 legacy
     save that M13, M14 and P2 each decompressed and rejected (one test with the union of
     their assertions). Version checks that sit inside a subsystem's own round-trip test
     (M4b, M9, M10, E6a, E6b) stay there.
   - The combined five-bundle province catalogue test (four copies) and the
     partial-ownership save round trips (six copies) are asserted once, in
     `map/provinces-oceania.test.ts`; that file gained the Americas copy's lower bound
     and a per-cut-off upper bound so no copy's assertion is lost.
   - Same-subject files were joined: No-Mana into `architecture/isolation`; M13 and M14
     into `architecture/naming-audits`; the six SVG fallback-map files into
     `map/svg-fallback`; four app source-wiring files into `ui-shell/app-wiring`; the
     worker protocol files into `worker/protocol`; and the pairs for public debt,
     economic reporting, map labels and the historical-data loader.
   - The drift test builds the 12 MB worker bundle three times instead of four
     (`--check` plus one independent generation, each equal to the committed file) and
     compares bytes with `Buffer.equals` instead of Jest's element-wise equality.
3. **Not merged, deliberately** (deviations from the proposal):
   - The five province files stay one per continent (renamed
     `map/provinces-<continent>.test.ts`). The proposal estimated ≈ 2,700 duplicated
     lines; measured, almost every same-titled test asserts on its own continent's
     provinces, and only the two groups above were true duplicates.
   - The military audit-remediation files and the two death-allocation files keep their
     own files (renamed by subject): their helpers and constants have the same names as
     the neighbouring suite's but different definitions, so a merge would have meant
     rewriting test internals.
   - Fixture files keep their milestone names (`m5.ts`, `m3d.ts`, …): their exports and
     this register name them that way ("the M5 sample", "the M3 fixture").
   - The M11b monogram test stays in `data/catalogue-coverage`; only one of its lines
     repeats `data/flags`.
4. **Benchmarks.** `packages/bench/src/p1WorldBench.ts` and the root `bench:world` script
   are removed: `perfBench.ts` runs the same staged-versus-phase cross-check on the same
   world, and `determinism/rollback` asserts it in the suite.
5. **Documents.** Historical records move unchanged to `docs/archive/` (only relative
   links were updated): the 18 GDD editions v2–v2.17 (`gdd/`), the 18 GDD change reports
   (`gdd-change-reports/`), 13 completion reports (`completion-reports/`; P4 and P5 stay
   at the root until accepted), 13 milestone records (`milestones/`: requirements maps,
   the M11 design record and playtest guide, the M11d and M11e reports, the M13 and M14
   rename reports, the P2b calibration plan), and the former README as
   `README_HISTORY.md`. Nothing historical was deleted (the designer chose to archive the
   old GDD editions, not delete them).
   - `README.md` is rewritten as a current overview (documents, workspace, commands,
     test layout, determinism and saves, workflow).
   - Refreshed current documents: `docs/HISTORICAL_DATA_PROVENANCE.md` (the 1900 world
     after P2: provinces and customs territories instead of regions),
     `docs/M11_SLIDER_INVENTORY.md` (current labels, province recruitment limit, money
     sliders in display units since P3), the `docs/PROSPERITY_PROGRAM.md` status line, and
     `docs/FLAG_PROVENANCE.md`, whose generator (`scripts/m11/build-flags.mjs`) carried
     41 mis-encoded dashes in its string literals; the document was regenerated with
     `--emit`, and the two generated TS outputs are unchanged.
   - The GDD's two file references to the v2.12 and v2.13 change reports, and six file
     references in D-135, D-137, D-139 and D-142, now name the archive paths. No entry was
     removed, renumbered or otherwise reworded.
   - Both terminology audits treat `docs/archive/` as historical and point at the moved
     M13/M14 reports.
6. **Decision index.** The register opens with an index generated by
   `scripts/docs/decisions-index.mjs`; `--check` (run by `architecture/generated-drift`)
   fails if the index is stale or a decision number is duplicated or missing. (The first
   version accepted the mis-encoded heading dashes of D-101 and D-102; see the follow-up
   below.)

### Consequences

- Tests: 115 files (40,691 lines) in one folder became 98 files (40,335 lines) in twelve
  folders; 1,397 tests became 1,386 (1,387 with the new decision-index test), all passing, with every removed test accounted for
  title by title (eight version and legacy-save tests became six table rows and tests;
  nine duplicate province tests went, one was renamed). Fixtures are unchanged (24 files).
  Benchmark entry points: 23 → 22.
- Documents: 55 root Markdown files (105,252 lines) became 6 (the four current documents
  and the P4 and P5 reports under review); 64 files are in `docs/archive/`; `docs/` keeps
  21 current documents plus `CLEANUP_PROPOSAL.md`. README went from 3,470 lines to 132.
- The in-band suite took 1,741 s in the baseline and 1,362–1,464 s in the six full runs after the
  drift-test change (one machine, Node 24.22.1), mostly from that change.
- The suite's in-band Jest process grows with every suite it loads (Jest's
  `--experimental-vm-modules` keeps module graphs alive) and the baseline already ran
  close to its 4 GB heap: moving the files changed Jest's run order, the drift test ran
  last instead of first, and the run ran out of memory until that test stopped deep-
  comparing 12 MB buffers. Fewer suites now load fewer copies of the module graph, but
  the headroom is limited; a future change that adds a large suite may need a larger
  heap or Jest workers.
### Follow-up (2026-11-06, at the designer's request)

The items the cleanup first left open were then resolved as follows:

- **Stray files.** `.cache-regions.md` and `.cache-regions-utf8.md` (UTF-16 dumps of the
  pre-P2 region list, committed by accident in `11a9926`) are deleted; git history keeps
  them.
- **D-144 test.** `population/fertility-ceiling.test.ts` (12 tests) covers the
  natural-fertility saturation as D-144 specifies it: identity to the knee, slope 1 there,
  strictly increasing and never above the cap, D-144's known answers (raw 2.5 → 2.222;
  the 1993 block's raw 4.009 → 2.2995), the exact inverse used to fit residuals, every
  sourced 1900 level at or below the knee, and a block whose causal product passes the
  old bound staying valid. It adds tests only; D-144's status line is unchanged, and the
  designer's revision of the P4 tests may fold it in.
- **Mis-encoded text.**
  - `DECISIONS.md`: the five mis-encoded dashes in D-101 and D-102 (both headings and
    three list items) are repaired. The words are unchanged, and the index script now
    accepts only the plain dash.
  - Authored sources: repaired in `scripts/m11/boundaries-1900-source.mjs` (33
    descriptions and notes, plus comments), the comments of
    `scripts/m12c/build-africa-provinces.mjs`, and a transfer reason in
    `map/provinces-americas.test.ts`.
  - The archived README's link to the deleted `regions_1900.json` is now plain text
    naming the P2 commit that removed it.
- **Still open: the 1900 map outputs.** `boundaries_1900.json`, `world_map_1900.json`,
  `worldMap1900.ts` and `docs/HISTORICAL_MAP_PROVENANCE.md` keep their mis-encoded
  names and notes (for example `KlaipÄ—da`). They were not regenerated because the
  pipeline no longer reproduces them. `scripts/m11/build-boundaries.mjs` writes
  `m11c-1900-boundaries-v1` without the two `split-by-current-borders` dispute
  resolutions that commit `1a2a68a` added to the committed `m11d-1900-boundaries-v2`
  data, and no script in the repository produces them. Regenerating would therefore
  regress the map. Hand-editing the generated outputs is not allowed, so they wait for
  the M11d resolution step to be restored to the map pipeline, a map-data change of its
  own. The designer chose to leave it for now. `docs/MAP_DATA.md` §7,
  `docs/HISTORICAL_DATA_PROVENANCE.md` §6 and the header of `build-boundaries.mjs` now
  warn against running the map steps until then.
- **Other generated documents.** The provincialization documents are also stale against
  their generators. Rerun on the current data, the Europe, Asia and Africa generators
  give different counts and lists (for example Europe 569 provinces, not 570), and the
  Oceania generator stops with a TypeError. The documents were therefore not
  regenerated.
- **Stale test names.** Ten stale test-file references in current documents were
  missed in step 6 and are now corrected: `MAP_DATA`, `MAP_RENDERING`,
  `FONT_PROVENANCE`, `HISTORICAL_DATA_PROVENANCE`, `HISTORICAL_MAP_PROVENANCE` and the
  four provincialization documents. The generator templates were corrected to match,
  and each document had only that line edited. The GDD still names about 20 old test
  files in its milestone acceptance sections. They are left as written, because GDD
  edits need the designer.
